# People of 1861–62: sources and evidence

Companion to `people.csv` and `README.md` in this folder. Part 1 lists the works cited and their public-domain status (decision 0033: facts of record come only from public-domain sources). Part 2 resolves every citation key, group by group. Part 3 gives the evidence behind each stat direction and trait, person by person.

## Part 1. Works cited

### How keys are formed

| Key form | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `HEIT1-<id>`, `HEIT2-p<n>` | Heitman, vol. 1 entry for the person; vol. 2 page |
| `CULLUM-<n>` | Cullum's Register, graduate number n (the same in every edition) |
| `WRIGHT-p<n>` | Wright (1911), page |
| `BDAC-<id>` | Biographical Directory of the American Congress (1928), entry for the person |
| `CALLAHAN-<id>` | Callahan's navy list (1901), entry for the officer |
| `APPLETONS-<id>`, `DNB-<id>` | Appletons' Cyclopaedia, Dictionary of National Biography, entry for the person |
| `OR-<series>-<vol>[-<part>]-p<n>` | Official Records (Army), e.g. `OR-I-11-1-p61` is Series I, vol. 11, part 1, p. 61 |
| `NH-<vol>-p<n>` | Nicolay and Hay, *Abraham Lincoln: A History* (1890) |
| `WELLES-D-<date>`, `HAY-D-<date>`, `CHASE-D-<date>`, `JONES-<vol>-<date>` | diary entries by date |
| `GRANT-PM-ch<n>`, `SHERMAN-M-ch<n>`, `MCCLELLAN-OS-p<n>`, `BL-<vol>-p<n>` and similar | memoirs by chapter or page |
| `LINCOLN-<date>-<recipient>` | a Lincoln letter; Part 2 says where the text was read |
| `NOTE-<name>` | one of the project's research notes in `docs/research/`; a pointer to a primary document, never the source of a fact |

### The works

| Work | Keys | Public-domain status | Read as |
|---|---|---|---|
| F. B. Heitman, *Historical Register and Dictionary of the United States Army, 1789–1903* (GPO, 1903), vols 1–2 | `HEIT1`, `HEIT2` | Public domain (published under act of Congress, 1903) | archive.org `historicalregist01heitrich`, `historicalregist02heitrich` (OCR) |
| G. W. Cullum, *Biographical Register of the Officers and Graduates of the U.S. Military Academy*, 2nd ed. (1879) vols 1–2 and 3rd ed. (1891) | `CULLUM` | Public domain (1879, 1891) | archive.org `biographicalregi01cull`, `biographicalregi02cull`, `biographicalregi03bost` (OCR); Thayer's transcription of the 3rd ed. at penelope.uchicago.edu |
| Marcus J. Wright, *General Officers of the Confederate Army, Officers of the Executive Departments of the Confederate States, Members of the Confederate Congress by States* (Neale, 1911) | `WRIGHT` | Public domain (1911) | archive.org `generalofficers00wrigrich` (OCR) |
| *Biographical Directory of the American Congress, 1774–1927* (GPO, 1928) | `BDAC` | Public domain (US government work) | archive.org `in.ernet.dli.2015.74142` (OCR; digits often garbled) |
| E. W. Callahan, *List of Officers of the Navy of the United States and of the Marine Corps, 1775–1900* (1901) | `CALLAHAN` | Public domain (Navy Department compilation, 1901) | archive.org `listofofficersof00unit` |
| *Congressional Directory*, 37th Congress, 2nd session (1861) | `CONGDIR37` | Public domain (US government) | archive.org `congressionaldirunit` |
| *The War of the Rebellion: Official Records of the Union and Confederate Armies* (GPO, 1880–1901) | `OR` | Public domain (US government) | civilwar.com page transcriptions and archive.org OCR (`warofrebellion…`); page numbers from running heads, can drift by one page; not collated with the print |
| *Report of the Joint Committee on the Conduct of the War* (1863), parts 1–3 | `JCCW-1863` | Public domain (US government) | archive.org `reportjointcomm01wargoog`, `reportjointcomm03wargoog` |
| House of Representatives, 37th Congress, 2nd session, Report No. 2, *Government Contracts* (1862) | `HR-37-2-2` | Public domain (US government) | archive.org `governmentcontra00wash` |
| *Papers Relating to Foreign Affairs* (December 1861 and December 1862 volumes) | `PRFA` | Public domain (US government) | archive.org `papersrelatingto00unit_0`, `papersrelatingt00unit` |
| *Journal of the Congress of the Confederate States of America, 1861–1865* (GPO, 1904–05) | `JC` | Public domain (US government) | archive.org `journalofcongres00conf`, `journalofcongres02confiala` |
| J. D. Richardson, ed., *A Compilation of the Messages and Papers of the Confederacy* (1905), vol. 2 | `RICH` | Public domain (1905) | archive.org `compilationofmes02conf`; cited by dispatch date (no reliable page numbers in the OCR) |
| Frank Moore, ed., *The Rebellion Record*, vol. 2 (1862) | `REBREC` | Public domain (1862) | archive.org `rebellionrecord0002fran` |
| *Appletons' Cyclopaedia of American Biography* (1887–89), vols 1–6 | `APPLETONS` | Public domain (1887–89) | archive.org `appletonscyclop01wilsuoft` and companions; Wikisource. **Period but secondary: an Appletons-only direction is flagged `[single source]`** |
| *Dictionary of National Biography* (1885–1900) | `DNB` | Public domain | Wikisource |
| *Diary of Gideon Welles* (1911), vol. 1 | `WELLES-D`, `WELLES-D1` | Public domain (1911) | archive.org `diaryofgideonwel0001unse_i9v3`. **Welles's view; hostile to Seward, Chase and Stanton** |
| *Letters of John Hay and Extracts from Diary* (1908), vol. 1 | `HAY-D`, `HAY-D1` | Public domain (published 1908) | archive.org `lettersofjohnhay01hayj` |
| S. P. Chase, *Diary and Correspondence* (Annual Report of the American Historical Association for 1902, vol. 2, 1903) | `CHASE-D` | Public domain (1903) | archive.org |
| J. G. Nicolay and J. Hay, *Abraham Lincoln: A History* (Century, 1890) | `NH` | Public domain (1890) | archive.org `abrahamlincolnhi03nico`, `johnnicolayabe03nicorich` and companions. **Lincoln's secretaries: own-side** |
| Memoirs of participants: Grant, *Personal Memoirs* (1885–86); Sherman, *Memoirs* (1875); McClellan, *McClellan's Own Story* (1887); J. E. Johnston, *Narrative of Military Operations* (1874); J. Davis, *Rise and Fall of the Confederate Government* (1881); Longstreet, *From Manassas to Appomattox* (1896); Julian, *Political Recollections* (1884); S. S. Cox, *Union–Disunion–Reunion* (1885); Greeley, *Recollections of a Busy Life* (1868); Dana, *Recollections of the Civil War* (1898); Weed, *Autobiography* (1883) and Barnes, *Memoir* (1884); Bulloch, *The Secret Service of the Confederate States in Europe* (1883) | `GRANT-PM`, `SHERMAN-M`, `MCCLELLAN-OS`, `JOHNSTON-1874`, `DAVIS-RF`, `LONGSTREET-1896`, `JULIAN-PR`, `COX-3DEC`, `GREELEY-RBL`, `DANA-RCW`, `WEED-MEM`, `BULLOCH` | Public domain (published before 1929) | Gutenberg 4367 and 4361; archive.org. **Each flagged `[own memoir]` or `[rival memoir]`; Johnston's and Davis's are advocacy, cited only for what the author claimed** |
| *Battles and Leaders of the Civil War* (Century, 1887–88), vols 1–2 | `BL` | Public domain (1887–88) | archive.org `bwb_S0-BUN-264_1`, `battlesleadersof02cent`. **Participants' articles: memoirs, author named** |
| J. B. Jones, *A Rebel War Clerk's Diary* (1866); M. B. Chesnut, *A Diary from Dixie* (1905 edition) | `JONES`, `CHESNUT` | Public domain | archive.org `arebelwarclerks01jonegoog`, `diaryfromdixieas00chesiala`. **Jones is hostile to Benjamin and Cooper; the 1905 Chesnut text was revised** |
| Admiring biographies and family compilations: Riddle, *Life of Benjamin F. Wade* (1886); Pierce, *Memoir and Letters of Charles Sumner* (1893); Nason and Russell, *Henry Wilson* (1876); *Zachariah Chandler: An Outline Sketch* (1880); Woodburn, *Life of Thaddeus Stevens* (1913); Foulke, *Life of Oliver P. Morton* (1899); H. G. Pearson, *Life of John A. Andrew* (1904); Lord Newton, *Lord Lyons* (1913); Jordan and Pryor, *Campaigns of N. B. Forrest* (1868); R. E. Lee Jr., *Recollections and Letters of General Robert E. Lee* (1904) | `RIDDLE-WADE`, `PIERCE-SUMNER`, `NASON-WILSON`, `CHANDLER-OS`, `WOODBURN-STEVENS`, `FOULKE-MORTON`, `PEARSON-ANDREW`, `NEWTON-LYONS`, `JORDAN-PRYOR-1868`, `LEE-1904` | Public domain (published before 1929) | archive.org; Gutenberg 43317 (Newton). **Partisan: flagged where used** |
| A. H. Stephens, speech at Savannah, 21 March 1861 (the "Cornerstone" speech), newspaper report | `STEPHENS` | Public domain (1861) | a transcription of the period newspaper text |
| *Diary of Edward Bates, 1859–1866* (Annual Report of the American Historical Association for 1930, vol. 4, GPO 1933) | `BATES-D` | **Unconfirmed**: published after 1929, no stated licence | archive.org `diaryofedwardbat00bate`. **Consulted only: no direction rests on it alone** |

Not reached: the Navy Official Records, the Congressional Globe, Lincoln's *Collected Works* (refused automated access), the 1860 census slave schedules.

## Part 2. Citation keys, by group

Each group's table lists every key its rows use, with the full reference, its public-domain status and where it was read. "corpus/" names a file in the lane's downloaded corpus; Part 1 maps the main files to their archive.org or Gutenberg identifiers.

Notes: Bates's diary (1933 GPO, AHA Annual Report vol. IV) is a federal publication, but the diary manuscript belonged to Helen Nicolay and the editor's notes are Howard K. Beale's. archive.org metadata gives no copyright status. I rely only on Bates's own text and on date/page references, never on the editor's verdicts. Project must confirm before treating it as a source of record. Local OCR of Appletons and BDAC has no reliable page numbers and some misread digits (flagged where it matters). new downloads saved under corpus/: bates-1933-diary.txt, chase-1903-diary-correspondence.txt, nicolay-hay-1890-v3..v6.txt, governmentcontra00wash.txt (and two small House documents governmentcontra00unit_1.txt, governmentcontra00dawe.txt, not cited), appletons-1900-fox-gustavus-vasa-wikisource.txt. The appletons-1887-v1..v6.txt files were already in corpus/ when I looked (added by another helper); I checked their title pages as Appletons' Cyclopaedia.

### Union generals: the East (`union_east`)

All local files are under `corpus/` unless stated. OR page numbers were read from the running headers in the OCR (OCR can misprint a header; each cited page was checked against the headers before and after the passage). Welles and Hay dates are the diary entry dates; Hay page numbers are of the 1908 volume.

Downloads added to corpus/ for this group (all archive.org full text, public domain): lifeofbenjaminfw00inridd, politicalrecolle00juliiala, congressionaldirunit, reportjointcomm01wargoog (JCCW Part 2), reportjointcomm02wadegoog and reportjointcomm03wargoog (both Part 3), zachariahchandle00pier, lifeofthaddeusst00wooduoft, memoirlettersofc04pieruoft, lifeofclementlva00vallrich (not used), lifepublic00nason, uniondisunionreu00coxs, journalisminunit00hudsuoft (searched only), rebellionrecord0002fran. Nicolay and Hay vols 4-6 and the Appletons volumes were placed in the corpus by another lane; their archive.org identifiers were not recorded here.

Not available or not read: Congressional Globe (37th Congress, sessions 1-3), JCCW Part 1 (Army of the Potomac), the OR, Wikisource copies of Lincoln's collected works. Everything that needs them is marked needs-source.

#### Files downloaded in this lane (under corpus/)

- or-i-01.txt (OR S.I vol 1, archive warofrebellionco0001vari); or-i-02.txt (S.I vol 2, warofrebellionco0002vari); or-i-05.txt, or-i-06.txt, or-i-07.txt, or-i-10-1.txt, or-i-10-2.txt, or-i-11-1.txt, or-i-11-2.txt, or-i-11-3.txt, or-i-12-1.txt, or-i-12-2.txt, or-i-12-3.txt, or-i-19-1.txt, or-i-19-2.txt, or-i-21.txt, or-i-51-2.txt (archive.org full text).
- or-ii-1.txt and or-iii-2.txt: Series II vol 1 and Series III vol 2, downloaded by mistake under the names or-i-01 and or-i-02 and since renamed; do not read them as Series I.
- or-i-12-2.txt is the Part II file of vol 12 as scanned (Fitz John Porter court-martial); the Second Manassas Confederate reports were not obtained.
- lee-1904-recollections-and-letters.txt, johnston-1874-narrative.txt, longstreet-1896-manassas-to-appomattox.txt.

Not used as evidence: modern works. NOTE-confederate-side and NOTE-march-1861-facts (project research notes) were read only as pointers.

### Confederate generals: the West (`confederate_west`)

Page note: OR, B&L and Cullum text are OCR; page numbers are read from running headers (a passage between two headers is cited at the nearest preceding header, so one page of error is possible). Local files are in `corpus/`. Files marked "(shared)" were present from other lanes; I downloaded or-i-03, or-i-04, or-i-06, or-i-08, or-i-15, or-i-16-1, or-i-17-1 and jordan-pryor-1868-forrest.txt from archive.org (and or-i-52-1, or-i-16-2, not used).

## Part 3. Evidence, by person

One section per person, in each group's priority order. Flags: `[single source]`, `[own memoir]`, `[rival memoir: author]`, `[contested]`. A stat marked `unknown` says what was searched.

### Union cabinet, White House and War and Navy Department bureaus (`union_executive`)

Flags: [own memoir], [rival memoir: <author>], [single source], [contested]. NH = Nicolay and Hay, written by Lincoln's secretaries, so every NH line is [own memoir] on Lincoln, Nicolay and Hay. Page numbers come from OCR running headers.

#### lincoln_abraham: Abraham Lincoln
- competence above [contested]: On 1 Apr 1861 he answered Seward's "Some thoughts" memorandum in a short letter: the inaugural already stated the policy, and "if this must be done, I must do it" (NH-3-p448, NH-3-p449) [own memoir: Lincoln's secretaries print it from the manuscript]. He cut Cameron's report on arming slaves and replaced the paragraph (NH-5-p125, NH-5-p126), interlined a limit ("not to mean a general arming of them") into the War Department's Port Royal instruction (NH-5-p124), and wrote Fremont to modify his proclamation (NH-4-p418). Against: Welles records Lincoln saying he signed the 1 Apr 1861 navy papers without reading because he trusted Seward (WELLES-D1-p18) [rival memoir: Welles, written years later]; Bates found him in "painful and mortyfying doubt" over removing Fremont (BATES-D-1861-10-22) [single contemporary diary].
- nerve above [contested]: Same 1 Apr 1861 letter (NH-3-p448). After the Cabinet changed its vote he ordered the Sumter expedition prepared (NH-3-p433). Against: the Fremont delay (BATES-D-1861-10-22).
- experience below: Four terms in the Illinois legislature from 1834 and a volunteer captaincy in 1832 (APPLETONS-lincoln_abraham; HEIT1-lincoln_abraham); no executive office or army command before March 1861.
- reputation_1861 average: Nominated by the 1860 convention and inaugurated 4 Mar 1861 (HEIT1-lincoln_abraham); no press or contemporary verdict read, so needs-source for anything stronger.
- candid: Asked "What have I done wrong?" and told Welles the 1 Apr papers were not really his instructions and should be treated as cancelled (WELLES-D1-p17, WELLES-D1-p18) [rival memoir: Welles].
- antislavery: Wrote to Greeley on 22 Aug 1862 of his "oft-expressed personal wish that all men everywhere could be free", while stating the Union as his paramount object (NH-6-p153; also NH-6-p152).
- secretive: The 1 Apr 1861 Pickens orders were kept from the Secretaries of War and Navy by design, "an error" Nicolay and Hay say Lincoln never repeated (NH-3-p439) [own memoir]; Welles confirms Lincoln signed them (WELLES-D1-p17).

#### seward_william_h: William H. Seward
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): APPLETONS-seward_william_h, BATES-D-1862-02-02
- competence above [contested]: His Trent dispatch of 26 Dec 1861 conceded release on a legal argument about the missing prize-court step while keeping American principle (NH-5-p38, NH-5-p39); the Cabinet examined his draft on 25 Dec (NH-5-p35) and Bates records urging the release (NH-5-p36) [Bates's diary quoted in Nicolay and Hay]. Against: his 1 Apr 1861 memorandum proposed forcing Spain and France to explain or face war and shifting the issue to Union against disunion (NH-3-p445, NH-3-p446, NH-3-p447); Lincoln's reply rebuts it (NH-3-p448).
- nerve average: Advised against the Sumter expedition and for evacuation on 29 Mar 1861 but for holding Pickens at the risk of war (NH-3-p430). Mixed: bold on paper in April, cautious on Sumter.
- experience high: Governor of New York 1839-42 and Senator 1849-61 (BDAC-seward_william_h).
- reputation_1861 high: "One of the most prominent aspirants" for the 1860 Republican nomination (BDAC-seward_william_h).
- secretive: Managed the Pickens orders with Meigs and Porter without the War and Navy Secretaries (NH-3-p439); Welles says Seward spent his time at the Executive Mansion and "less was known of the transactions of the State Department" (WELLES-D1-p14) [rival memoir: Welles].
- makes_policy: On 5 Feb 1862 Bates complained that Seward directed a US marshal to move piracy prisoners to Fort Lafayette without the Attorney General's knowledge (BATES-D-1862-02-05) [single source; political arrests are a sensitive-history item, record neutrally].
- intrigues: Welles says Seward worked against the Sumter relief plan and with Porter and Barron on Pickens (WELLES-D1-p9, WELLES-D1-p17) [rival memoir: Welles, openly one-sided about Seward].

#### chase_salmon_p: Salmon P. Chase
- competence above: On 15 Nov 1861 he hurried to New York to sell Government bonds and telegraphed that credit was rising (BATES-D-1861-11-15). His own diary says that after losing confidence in McClellan he raised means and urged reinforcements (CHASE-D-1862-09-02) [own memoir]. Finance record 1861-62 beyond this needs-source.
- nerve above: Wrote on 29 Mar 1861 that provisioning Sumter should be attempted even if war resulted (NH-3-p430); on 1 Sep 1862 he and Stanton, Bates and Smith signed a remonstrance against McClellan (CHASE-D-1862-09-01) [own memoir]; on 20 Dec 1862 he offered his resignation in front of colleagues (NH-6-p267, NH-6-p268) [own memoir: Lincoln's secretaries].
- experience above: Senator 1849-55, Governor of Ohio from 1856, Senator-elect 1860; no financial office before (BDAC-chase_salmon_p).
- reputation_1861 high: Governor twice, re-elected Senator 1860, member of the 1861 peace convention (BDAC-chase_salmon_p).
- antislavery: Liberty Party member from 1841, Free-Soil convention 1848 (BDAC-chase_salmon_p; APPLETONS-chase_salmon_p).
- proposed trait: Chase told Bates on 14 Mar 1862 that he had worked two months to bring Stanton into the War Department (BATES-D-1862-03-14); this is evidence for a patronage sponsor, recorded under Stanton.

#### cameron_simon: Simon Cameron
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): APPLETONS-cameron_simon, BATES-D-1862-01-13, NH-5-p128
- competence below [contested]: The House Select Committee on Government Contracts (report of 17 Dec 1861) shows the War Secretary on 21-23 Apr 1861 giving a newspaper publisher, Alexander Cummings, authority to buy supplies and joint control over funds, from which Cummings drew $250,000 and put $160,000 to his own bank credit (HR-37-2-2-p56, HR-37-2-2-p57, HR-37-2-2-p58). The House censured him in April 1862 for a policy "highly injurious to the public service"; Lincoln told the House the acts were his and the Cabinet's (NH-5-p129, NH-5-p130) [own memoir: Lincoln's secretaries; secondhand]. Welles says he went partly over "loose matters of contracts" and lack of grasp (WELLES-D1-p57) [rival memoir: Welles]. Bates, 2 Feb 1862: "I do not judge his case" (BATES-D-1862-02-02).
- nerve above: His Dec 1861 report argued it was "as clearly a right of the Government to arm slaves ... as it is to use gunpowder taken from the enemy" (NH-5-p125, NH-5-p126) and Bates saw him urging the arming of Black soldiers in Cabinet (BATES-D-1861-11-20) [single contemporary diary].
- experience average: Senator 1845-49 and 1857-61, state adjutant general, bank cashier and railroad president (BDAC-cameron_simon); no war-office experience.
- reputation_1861 below [contested]: Strong Pennsylvania support for the 1860 nomination but no Vice-Presidential nomination (BDAC-cameron_simon); Chicago delegates called him "a slippery fellow, a political gambler" (BATES-D-1860-05-19, a marginal note reporting Goodrich) [single source, hearsay].
- contract_scandal: Charge, not proven enrichment: committee criticism of the Cummings arrangement (HR-37-2-2-p57) and the House censure (NH-5-p129).
- poor_administrator: The committee describes funds and purchases placed with an agent "exhibiting ... a confiding disposition hardly consistent with a thorough business man" (HR-37-2-2-p58).
- makes_policy: His annual report took a stand on arming slaves and was printed and mailed to postmasters before Lincoln saw it (NH-5-p125).

#### stanton_edwin_m: Edwin M. Stanton
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): BATES-D-1862-01-13, CHASE-D-1862-09-01, NH-5-p131
- competence above [contested]: Lincoln's secretaries say he banished "shoulder-straps" from the capital, put the telegraph under the War Department and held public hours for applicants (NH-5-p141) [own memoir]. Welles gives him hearty support for the New Orleans expedition (WELLES-D1-p60) but says he was the most frightened man in the government on 9 Mar 1862 (WELLES-D1-p62) [rival memoir: Welles, one-sided about Stanton]. 1862 contracts and recruiting record needs-source (OR Series III).
- nerve above [contested]: Appletons says that in Buchanan's Cabinet he called surrender of Sumter a crime and that Floyd resigned afterwards (APPLETONS-stanton_edwin_m) [single source]; Nicolay and Hay say he disclosed Cabinet facts to Republican leaders (NH-5-p132) [own memoir]. Against: Welles on 9 Mar 1862 (WELLES-D1-p62, WELLES-D1-p63).
- experience above: Leading lawyer before the Supreme Court; Ohio reporter 1842-45; Buchanan's Attorney General from 20 Dec 1860 (APPLETONS-stanton_edwin_m); no executive office before then.
- reputation_1861 average: Welles says Republicans did not count him as one of their own and he had the name of an anti-secession Democrat; Seward and Chase vouched for him in 1861 (WELLES-D1-p55, WELLES-D1-p57) [rival memoir: Welles].
- organiser: NH-5-p141 above [own memoir].
- disdains_superiors: Nicolay and Hay quote his confidential 1861 letters: "no high opinion of Mr. Lincoln", the "trepidation of Lincoln", and after Bull Run the "painful imbecility of Lincoln" (NH-5-p134, NH-5-p135) [own memoir: Lincoln's secretaries, hostile witness against their own side]. Welles adds that he ridiculed the President and "freely expressed" judgement against public measures (WELLES-D1-p55) [rival memoir: Welles; rumour].
- political_patron: Chase told Bates on 14 Mar 1862 that he had worked two months to bring him in (BATES-D-1862-03-14); Welles says Seward pressed him for District Attorney in 1861 (WELLES-D1-p55) [rival memoir].

#### welles_gideon: Gideon Welles
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): CHASE-D-1862-09-01, CHASE-D-1862-09-02, WELLES-D1-p15
- competence above [contested]: Bates called him honest and faithful but "not strong or quick" (BATES-D-1862-02-02) [single contemporary diary]. Appletons credits his energy with a fleet rising from 42 to 82 vessels by 4 Jul 1861, report not read (APPLETONS-welles_gideon) [secondary, retrospective]. Lincoln told him on 15 Aug 1862 that his choice was fixed on Welles for New England from the morning after the election (WELLES-D-1862-08-15) [own memoir].
- nerve above: Wrote on 29 Mar 1861 for sending ships and provisions to Sumter and strengthening Pickens (NH-3-p431); says he was calm on 9 Mar 1862 and Lincoln later credited him for it (WELLES-D1-p62) [own memoir, single source].
- experience average: Chief of the Navy's Bureau of Provisions and Clothing 1846-49; state comptroller; no seagoing or fleet experience (APPLETONS-welles_gideon).
- reputation_1861 average: Chosen as the New England member, not a naval figure (WELLES-D-1862-08-15; APPLETONS-welles_gideon).
- Needs-source: contemporary naval officers' judgements (ORN).

#### blair_montgomery: Montgomery Blair
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): HEIT1-blair_montgomery, WELLES-D1-p57
- competence above [single source]: Appletons credits him with money orders, free city delivery and postal railroad cars and with barring disloyal papers from the mails (APPLETONS-blair_montgomery); Bates reports the newspaper exclusion on 6 Dec 1861 (BATES-D-1861-12-06). He supported McClellan until Sept 1862, then agreed he could not be trusted with command (CHASE-D-1862-09-02) [own memoir: Chase's diary].
- nerve high: His 29 Mar 1861 memorandum said Sumter ought to be relieved "without reference to Pickens", called South Carolina "the head and front of this rebellion", and refused to share responsibility for the opposite course (NH-3-p432); Appletons says it is said he held his resignation on the issue (APPLETONS-blair_montgomery).
- experience above: West Point 1835 (CULLUM-803), St. Louis mayor 1842, judge 1843-49, US solicitor in the Court of Claims 1855-58 (APPLETONS-blair_montgomery).
- reputation_1861 average: Counsel for Dred Scott; Maryland Republican convention president 1860; son of Francis P. Blair (APPLETONS-blair_montgomery).
- makes_policy: Excluded disunion newspapers from the mails on his own authority (BATES-D-1861-12-06).

#### bates_edward: Edward Bates
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): CHASE-D-1862-09-02
- competence above: Wrote the first Cabinet summing-up on 29 Mar 1861, calling for Pickens and Key West to be reinforced and Sumter relieved or evacuated (NH-3-p432); on 10 Jan 1862 he advised Lincoln to return the western gunboats to the Navy Department with orders to co-operate with the army (BATES-D-1862-01-10) [own memoir]. His opinions as Attorney General need-source.
- nerve average: Hedged on Sumter ("either to evacuate or relieve it", NH-3-p432); told Lincoln to act without conditions in Oct 1861 (BATES-D-1861-10-22) [own memoir].
- experience high: US district attorney, Congressman 1827-29, state legislator, judge of the St. Louis land court 1853-56, presided at the 1856 Whig convention (BDAC-bates_edward; APPLETONS-bates_edward).
- reputation_1861 above: Candidate for the 1860 Republican nomination, offered the War Department by Fillmore in 1850 (BDAC-bates_edward; BATES-D-1860-05-19).
- opposes_emancipation: He and Smith opposed Cameron's proposal to arm Black soldiers in Cabinet, Nov 1861 (BATES-D-1861-11-20) [own memoir]. Limited to arming; colonisation views in the diary index not read.

#### smith_caleb_b: Caleb B. Smith
- competence unknown: Only his 29 Mar 1861 memorandum (evacuate Sumter, defend the other forts, blockade; NH-3-p431) and agreement against McClellan in Sept 1862 (CHASE-D-1862-09-02) found; Bates's index lists a rumour of a bribery indictment, which I did not read as a finding. Searched Bates, Chase diary, Welles, Nicolay and Hay.
- nerve unknown: same search.
- experience above: Speaker of the Indiana house 1836, Whig Congressman 1843-49, newspaper founder (BDAC-smith_caleb_b; APPLETONS-smith_caleb_b).
- reputation_1861 unknown: member of the 1861 peace convention only (BDAC-smith_caleb_b).
- opposes_emancipation: Opposed arming Black soldiers "vehemently" in Cabinet, Nov 1861 (BATES-D-1861-11-20) [single contemporary diary].

#### hamlin_hannibal: Hannibal Hamlin
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): APPLETONS-hamlin_hannibal
- competence unknown: no 1861-62 acts found; searched Bates, Welles, Chase diary, Nicolay-Hay (v3-v6), Hay letters. The Welles diary of 15 Aug 1862 lists him among those who confirmed Lincoln's choice of Welles (WELLES-D-1862-08-15).
- nerve unknown: same.
- experience high: Congressman 1843-47, Senator 1848-57 and 1857-61, Governor of Maine 1857 (BDAC-hamlin_hannibal).
- reputation_1861 average: Bates in May 1860 wrote that he was fit and popular in Maine but the ticket erred in overlooking Pennsylvania (BATES-D-1860-05-19) [single source].

#### thomas_lorenzo: Lorenzo Thomas
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): APPLETONS-thomas_lorenzo
- competence average [single source]: Bates called his Oct 1861 report on Fremont decisive (BATES-D-1861-10-22) [own memoir]. The House committee records Cameron's 14 Oct 1861 order to Fremont as sent through him (HR-37-2-2-p78). Missouri report itself needs-source (OR).
- nerve unknown: no 1861-62 act of risk or delay found in the local corpus.
- experience high: West Point 1823; Adjutant General's Office from 1833; chief of staff to Butler at Monterey and to Scott 1853-61 (CULLUM-342; HEIT1-thomas_lorenzo).
- reputation_1861 average: Colonel Adjutant General from 7 Mar 1861 and brevet brigadier on 7 May 1861 (CULLUM-342).

#### meigs_montgomery_c: Montgomery C. Meigs
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): WELLES-D1-p63
- competence high: Cullum credits him with directing the equipping and supplying of the armies from 15 May 1861 and the Pickens expedition (CULLUM-846); the 1864 brevet was for "distinguished and meritorious services" (HEIT1-meigs_montgomery_c). The House committee records him telling a quartermaster to disregard Fremont's orders contrary to the Secretary's of 14 Oct 1861 (HR-37-2-2-p78). Seward and Lincoln picked him in March 1861 to plan Pickens (NH-3-p434, NH-3-p436) [own memoir].
- nerve above [contested]: Said Pickens could be held "if the navy would do its duty" (NH-3-p434); went on the Pickens expedition 13-22 Apr 1861 (CULLUM-846). Against: Welles calls him and Dahlgren "cautious, not to say timid" on 9 Mar 1862 (WELLES-D1-p62) [rival memoir: Welles].
- experience above: Built Fort Delaware works, Potomac Aqueduct and the Capitol and Post Office extensions 1852-60 (CULLUM-846); no field command.
- reputation_1861 above: Consulted by Seward and the President on Pickens in March 1861 as the officer to be promoted (NH-3-p434).
- organiser: Quartermaster duties from 15 May 1861 and the 1864 brevet (CULLUM-846; HEIT1-meigs_montgomery_c) [single source type: register].

#### fox_gustavus_v: Gustavus V. Fox
- competence above [single source]: Appletons quotes a Cabinet member (unnamed, retrospective) calling him "the really able man of the administration" who planned New Orleans and had the honour of selecting Farragut (APPLETONS-fox_gustavus_v). Welles says Scott sent him to Sumter on 19 Mar 1861 and Lincoln accepted his volunteering (WELLES-D1-p15); Fox's own requisition for the expedition is brief and specific (NH-3-p433). Welles adds he "inadvertently and incautiously" told Stanton and Butler of the New Orleans plan (WELLES-D1-p60) [own memoir].
- nerve high: Visited Sumter in March 1861 and was confident of supplying and reinforcing it (WELLES-D1-p15); navy opinion put with him held that light vessels could cross the bar on a dark high tide against army opinion of near-certain destruction (BATES-D-1861-03-09) [own memoir; single source each].
- experience average: Navy officer 1838-56, lieutenant on resignation (APPLETONS-fox_gustavus_v); no command of a squadron.
- reputation_1861 average: Bates on 9 Mar 1861 says Fox "seems to be au fait in both nautical and military matters" (BATES-D-1861-03-09) [single source].
- organiser: NH-3-p433, APPLETONS-fox_gustavus_v.

#### scott_thomas_a: Thomas A. Scott
- competence above [single source]: Appletons says he opened the Washington-Philadelphia route via Annapolis "with surprising quickness" in April 1861 and took charge of government railways on 23 May (APPLETONS-scott_thomas_a); Cameron's 21 Apr 1861 letter names him to take charge of the railroads (HR-37-2-2-p56).
- nerve unknown.
- experience above: Pennsylvania Railroad since 1850, vice president 1859 (APPLETONS-scott_thomas_a).
- reputation_1861 unknown.
- organiser: APPLETONS-scott_thomas_a, HR-37-2-2-p56. Not found in Heitman vol 1; Bates Dec 1861 shows Cameron relying on "his Assistant Mr. Scott" (BATES-D-1861-12-25).

#### ripley_james_w: James W. Ripley
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): APPLETONS-ripley_james_w
- competence average [contested]: The House committee calls him "a gentleman of large experience and inexorable in the performance of his public duties" but notes he refused to alter the Hall carbines that were then sold and repurchased at a high price, calling the sale "utterly indefensible" if the arm was worth altering (HR-37-2-2-p41, HR-37-2-2-p42). His resistance to repeaters is needs-source (NOTE-union-war-economy is a pointer only).
- nerve unknown.
- experience high: Ordnance since 1832; Springfield Armory 1841-54; inspector of arsenals 1857-60 (CULLUM-102; HEIT1-ripley_james_w).
- reputation_1861 average: Colonel and Chief of Ordnance in April 1861, brigadier in August (CULLUM-102).

#### totten_joseph_g: Joseph G. Totten
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): APPLETONS-totten_joseph_g
- competence above: Chief Engineer from 1838; coast-defense board 1819-38; Vera Cruz brevet (CULLUM-10; HEIT1-totten_joseph_g). Chair of the 1861 boards on fortification ordnance and disabled officers (CULLUM-10). Scott and he judged Sumter beyond relief (WELLES-D1-p9; NH-3-p378) [rival memoir: Welles; Scott's letter in Nicolay and Hay].
- nerve unknown: his Sumter view was an engineering estimate; no hesitation documented.
- experience high: West Point 1805, War of 1812, 22 years as Chief Engineer (CULLUM-10).
- reputation_1861 high: Brevet brigadier for Vera Cruz, Smithsonian Regent, Scott's "distinguished Chief Engineer" (CULLUM-10; NH-3-p378); Seward spoke of him as one of two old men nobody would put on horseback (NH-3-p434) [Meigs's account in Nicolay and Hay].

#### holt_joseph: Joseph Holt
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): NOTE-union-war-economy
- competence above [single source]: Scott, quoting his early 1861 dealings, called Holt "a strong and loyal man" (NH-3-p134); Holt wrote Anderson on 16 Jan 1861 approving his restraint and saying firing on the Star of the West was an act of war (NH-3-p110) [Nicolay and Hay, own memoir]. As Judge Advocate General from 3 Sep 1862 (HEIT1-holt_joseph). Special Commission on ordnance 1862 needs-source.
- nerve high: Same two items; Appletons says he denounced Kentucky's neutrality (APPLETONS-holt_joseph).
- experience above: Commissioner of Patents 1857, Postmaster General 1859, Secretary of War from 18 Jan 1861 (APPLETONS-holt_joseph; HEIT1-holt_joseph).
- reputation_1861 above: Scott's judgement above; Holt was at Scott's meeting with Welles on 6 Mar 1861 (WELLES-D1-p4).

#### eckert_thomas_t: Thomas T. Eckert
- competence above [single source]: McClellan's report says the Army of the Potomac's telegraph under "Maj. Eckert" was constantly supplied to build lines when the army moved, under fire (MCCLELLAN-OS-report-eckert) [own memoir: McClellan's report; names him "Thomas J."].
- nerve unknown: not found; searched Welles, Bates, Chase, Hay, Nicolay-Hay.
- experience unknown: needs-source for pre-1862 career (Heitman gives only Ohio and the 1862 commission, HEIT1-eckert_thomas_t).
- reputation_1861 unknown.
- organiser: MCCLELLAN-OS-report-eckert [single source].

#### nicolay_john_g: John G. Nicolay
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): HAY-L1908-intro
- competence unknown: searched Welles, Bates, Hay letters, Chase. He appears as courier of Lincoln's package to Welles on 1 Apr 1861 (WELLES-D1-p16).
- nerve unknown.
- experience below: Newspaper proprietor and assistant to the Illinois Secretary of State from 1857 (APPLETONS-nicolay_john_g); aged 29.
- reputation_1861 unknown.

#### hay_john_m: John Hay
- register facts and context (dates, posts, ranks, prewar career, notes): HEIT1-hay_john_m
- competence unknown: searched his 1908 letters and diary extracts (names given as initials), Bates, Welles.
- nerve unknown.
- experience low: Brown University 1858, then a law student in Springfield; admitted to the bar 1861; aged 22 in March 1861 (HAY-L1908-intro; APPLETONS-hay_john_m).
- reputation_1861 unknown.

### Union generals: the East (`union_east`)

Register facts (born, died, rank dates, prewar, experience) rest on Heitman (HEIT1), Cullum (CULLUM), Appletons and BDAC; they are not repeated per line. "initials expanded" = Hay's printed diary uses initials, expanded here from the corps assignments in OR-I-5-p4.

#### scott_winfield: Winfield Scott
- competence above: Congress resolved its thanks and a gold medal for the 1847 Mexico City campaign ("valor, skill, and judicious conduct") (HEIT1-scott_winfield). Scott's 1861 advice on Sumter (relief impracticable, about 20,000 men needed) is recorded by Welles (WELLES-D1-p4-10) [own memoir: Welles, written years after] [single source]. Age 74 and infirm in 1861.
- nerve average: [contested]. Congress honoured the 1847 advance (HEIT1); but Welles and Blair on 1862-09-12 call his Anaconda approach a defensive error and say he was reluctant to enter Virginia (WELLES-D-1862-09-12) [rival memoir: Welles/Blair, contemporary diary].
- experience high: regular officer from 1808, general-in-chief from 1841 (HEIT1-scott_winfield).
- reputation_1861 high: gold medal and thanks of Congress (1814, 1848) (HEIT1-scott_winfield).
- infirm: General Orders 94 retires him "upon his own application" and speaks of his "personal affliction" (OR-I-5-p639); Hay saw him with the order on 1 Nov (HAY-D-1861-11-01).

#### mcclellan_george_b: George B. McClellan
- competence above: Lincoln told the cabinet he has "great powers of organization and discipline" and arranges combinations better than any general, "and there his usefulness ends" (WELLES-D-1862-09-12) [single source for the words]. On 7 Sep 1862 Lincoln: "I must have McClellan to reorganize the army" (WELLES-D-1862-09-07).
- nerve below: same Lincoln remark: "He can't go ahead, he can't strike a blow", and waited at Yorktown (WELLES-D-1862-09-12); Welles names his "slows" (WELLES-D-1862-09-07). Halleck's 6 Aug 1862 letter presses him to move (OR-I-11-1-p82).
- experience average: Mexican War junior engineer, company of sappers, Crimea commission, resigned 1857, railroad executive; largest command before 1861 a company (CULLUM-1273).
- reputation_1861 above: Ohio's major general of volunteers 23 Apr 1861, major general USA 14 May 1861; thanks of the House 16 Jul 1861 (HEIT1-mcclellan_george_b).
- slow: Welles 1862-09-12 and 09-07; Halleck urging action 1862-08-06 (OR-I-11-1-p82).
- organiser: Lincoln as above (WELLES-D-1862-09-12); Lincoln defended his deliberateness to the "Jacobin" visitors on 26 Oct 1861 (HAY-D-1861-10-26).
- overestimates_enemy: Halleck writes that McClellan and his officers estimated the enemy near Richmond at 200,000 (OR-I-11-1-p82). [single source] Comparison with Confederate strength needs-source.
- shifts_blame: 28 Jun 1862 telegram to Stanton: "You have done your best to sacrifice this army" (OR-I-11-1-p61).
- disdains_superiors: same telegram (OR-I-11-1-p61); Hay records him going to bed while the President waited, 13 Nov 1861, calling it "unparalleled insolence" (HAY-D-1861-11-13) [single source; Hay's interpretation].
- opposes_emancipation: Harrison's Landing letter: "forcible abolition of slavery should not be contemplated for a moment" (OR-I-11-1-p74).
- Conflict note, exact text: on McClellan-OS-p152 McClellan writes that Stanton, before taking office, "never spoke of the President in any other way than as the 'original gorilla,' and often said that Du Chaillu was a fool to wander all the way to Africa in search of what he could so easily have found at Springfield, Illinois." The words are McClellan's report of Stanton's talk [own memoir, ed. W. C. Prime 1887; rival of Stanton]. The repo note credits the phrase to McClellan in a letter to his wife of 17 Nov 1861; that letter is not in the corpus: needs-source. Both versions kept.

#### mcdowell_irvin: Irvin McDowell
- competence average: JCCW records the 21 Jul 1861 attack opening in success and ending in defeat, with the principal cause assigned to Patterson (JCCW-1863-2-p5) [Committee finding]; no performance evidence read for 1862 (Cedar Mountain not cited).
- nerve above: Hay, reporting a talk with Stanton, lists McDowell first among the "fighting Generals" put at the head of corps (HAY-D1-p53-54) [single source; initials expanded].
- experience average: 23 years' service, staff in Mexico (bvt capt, Buena Vista), no field command before 1861 (HEIT1-mcdowell_irvin; CULLUM-963).
- reputation_1861 average: one gallantry brevet (bvt capt, Buena Vista, "gal and mer con") (HEIT1-mcdowell_irvin), stated as a public honour; no press evidence read.
- candid: 24 Jun 1861 to Scott's staff: under 14,000 effective, "hardly fit" artillery, enemy 23,000-25,000 at Manassas (OR-I-2-p118).
- shifts_blame: his Bull Run report (OR-I-2-p316, 4 Aug 1861) lists late regiments, new wagons and teamsters, three-month men leaving, and that Patterson was to keep Johnston in the Valley and "this was not done" (OR-I-2-p324, p325).
- Court of inquiry (OR-I-12-1-p34 to p38): convened 21 Nov 1862; Headquarters answered that it knew of no charges (OR-I-12-1-p38). The finding was not located: needs-source.

#### butler_benjamin_f: Benjamin F. Butler
- competence average: Welles on 1862-12-29: "ability as a police magistrate" at Baltimore and New Orleans, no "exalted opinion of the military qualities"; Lincoln agreed on his civil skill (WELLES-D-1862-12-29) [single source].
- nerve above: refused to return fugitive slaves as "contraband of war"; occupied Baltimore with 900 men; hanged Mumford (APPLETONS-butler_benjamin_f) [secondary, PD].
- experience below: lawyer and state senator, militia brigadier, no regular service (APPLETONS; BDAC-butler_benjamin_f).
- reputation_1861 above: unsuccessful Democratic candidate for governor 1860; delegate to the 1860 conventions (APPLETONS; BDAC).
- makes_policy: 24-27 May 1861 letters to Scott: he took fugitives employed against the Union as service property and asked for direction (OR-II-1-p752, p754); Cameron, 30 May: "Your action ... is approved" and set the account rule (OR-II-1-p754, p755). The word "contraband" is not in these passages; it is Appletons' label.
- harsh_with_civilians: General Orders No. 28, 15 May 1862: a woman insulting an officer or soldier "shall be regarded and held liable to be treated as a woman of the town plying her avocation" (OR-I-15-p426). Mumford hanging and the consul funds: Appletons only (APPLETONS-butler_benjamin_f). Sensitive.

#### banks_nathaniel_p: Nathaniel P. Banks
- competence below: Blair said Banks "was no general, had no capacity for chief command", though an estimable officer under orders (WELLES-D-1862-09-12) [single source].
- nerve unknown: searched Welles, Heitman, Appletons, OR chronology; no act under pressure read.
- experience low: no military service before 16 May 1861 (HEIT1-banks_nathaniel_p; BDAC-banks_nathaniel_p).
- reputation_1861 high: Speaker of the US House (1856), governor of Massachusetts (BDAC-banks_nathaniel_p; APPLETONS-banks_nathaniel_p).
- Posts: V Corps 20 Mar, Dept of the Shenandoah 12 Apr (OR-I-12-1-p1, OR-I-12-1-p2); II Corps, Army of Virginia (OR-I-12-3-p435).

#### pope_john: John Pope
- competence average: Heitman and Cullum credit the capture of Island No. 10 (brevet major general 1865) (HEIT1-pope_john; CULLUM-1127); Lincoln, 7 Sep 1862, spoke of a design to "break down" Pope (WELLES-D-1862-09-07). Second Bull Run outcome not rated; needs-source.
- nerve above: advance on New Madrid and Island No. 10 (CULLUM-1127).
- experience above: 20 years' service, topographical work, Mexican War brevets, district commands in Missouri 1861 (CULLUM-1127).
- reputation_1861 above: two gallantry brevets (Monterey, Buena Vista) (HEIT1-pope_john), a public honour.
- boastful: 14 Jul 1862 address: from the West, "where we have always seen the backs of our enemies" (OR-I-12-3-p473).

#### burnside_ambrose_e: Ambrose E. Burnside
- competence average: commanded at the capture of Roanoke Island and the attack on New Bern, Feb-Mar 1862 (CULLUM-1348); Fredericksburg failed, "For the failure in the attack I am responsible" (OR-I-21-p67).
- nerve above: Roanoke Island landing and attack after a month of storms (OR-I-9-p80); Stanton's thanks for the "brilliant success" at New Bern, 22 Mar 1862 (OR-I-9-p207); at Fredericksburg he moved "rather against the opinion of the President, Secretary, and" Halleck (OR-I-21-p67). Refusal of command (below) is self-doubt, kept as a trait.
- experience below: six years' service, no war service, resigned 1853, colonel 1st R.I. Infantry May-Aug 1861 (HEIT1-burnside_ambrose_e).
- reputation_1861 unknown: searched OR I-2 (McDowell's report lists him as brigade commander) and I-9; the earliest public thanks found are of Mar 1862; no gallantry brevet.
- self_doubting: Lincoln told the cabinet, 12 Sep 1862, that Burnside declined command and "declared himself unequal to the position" (WELLES-D-1862-09-12) [single source].
- candid: his Fredericksburg report accepts responsibility (OR-I-21-p67).

#### patterson_robert: Robert Patterson
- competence below: JCCW finds the "principal cause" of the Bull Run defeat was his failure to hold Johnston in the Valley (JCCW-1863-2-p5) [Committee finding; contested by Patterson].
- nerve below: advanced to Bunker Hill, then moved away; troops' term expiry given as reason (JCCW-1863-2-p5).
- experience above: major general of volunteers in the Mexican War; commanded Pennsylvania militia (HEIT1-patterson_robert; APPLETONS-patterson_robert).
- reputation_1861 above: oldest major general by commission in the Union service (APPLETONS-patterson_robert).
- overestimates_enemy: he testified Johnston had 35,000-40,000 against the Committee's 12,000-15,000 (JCCW-1863-2-p5) [contested; Confederate returns unread].

#### stone_charles_p: Charles P. Stone
- competence unknown: JCCW records McClellan's 20 Oct telegram suggesting "a slight demonstration", Stone's orders to Devens, and the tree-row mistaken for a camp (JCCW-1863-2-p9, p10, p11). The Committee called the evidence on reinforcements "very contradictory" and refrained from an opinion (JCCW-1863-2-p17); it reported testimony that "seemed to impeach" his military capacity and loyalty, with no recommendation (JCCW-1863-2-p17, p18). Not rated.
- nerve unknown: searched JCCW and OR-I-5; no unambiguous act.
- experience average: Mexican War ordnance brevets, resigned 1856 (HEIT1-stone_charles_p).
- reputation_1861 above: two gallantry brevets (Molino del Rey, Chapultepec) (HEIT1-stone_charles_p), stated as a public honour; also named inspector-general of D.C. volunteers on 1 Jan 1861 (CULLUM-1237).
- organiser: "organizing and disciplining" the D.C. volunteers, Jan-Apr 1861 (CULLUM-1237).
- Sensitive: arrested 8-9 Feb 1862 and held in Fort Lafayette; Stone asked for the charges (OR-I-5-p342). McClellan wrote on 7 Sep 1862 that he had "no doubt as to the loyalty and devotion of General Stone" (OR-I-5-p342).

#### wool_john_e: John E. Wool
- competence above: Congress's 1854 thanks and sword for Buena Vista (HEIT1-wool_john_e); "saved Fortress Monroe by timely re-enforcements" (APPLETONS-wool_john_e) [single source for 1861 claim].
- nerve unknown: no 1861-62 act read (Norfolk 1862 not read).
- experience high: regular officer from 1812 (HEIT1-wool_john_e).
- reputation_1861 high: bvt major general; thanks of Congress (HEIT1-wool_john_e).

#### dix_john_a: John A. Dix
- competence above: appointed Treasury secretary at the bankers' request; credited with relieving the financial deadlock and holding Maryland in 1861 (APPLETONS-dix_john_a) [single source, secondary].
- nerve high: telegram of Jan 1861 ordering a mutinous revenue-cutter captain arrested and "If any one attempts to haul down the American flag, shoot him on the spot" (APPLETONS-dix_john_a) [single source; primary needs-source].
- experience above: army officer 1812-28, US Senator, Treasury secretary (BDAC-dix_john_a).
- reputation_1861 high: Cabinet office; he organised seventeen regiments (APPLETONS-dix_john_a).

#### hunter_david: David Hunter
- competence unknown: Halleck's 19 Feb 1862 praise for the Fort Donelson reinforcement is quoted only in Appletons (APPLETONS-hunter_david); the Charleston expedition failed (same).
- nerve above: General Orders No. 11 of 9 May 1862 declaring slaves free on his own authority (OR-I-14-p341); the 12 Apr 1862 order and the first black regiment are Appletons only (APPLETONS-hunter_david).
- experience above: USMA 1822, dragoon captain, paymaster; commanded a division at Bull Run (CULLUM-310).
- reputation_1861 unknown: companion of Lincoln on the Feb 1861 journey (Appletons); no press evidence.
- makes_policy / antislavery: General Orders No. 11, 9 May 1862: martial law in Georgia, Florida and South Carolina; "Slavery and martial law in a free country are altogether incompatible"; slaves "declared forever free" (OR-I-14-p341). Lincoln's proclamation of 19 May 1862: no commander authorised, order "altogether void" in that respect, question reserved to himself (OR-II-1-p818).

#### sumner_edwin_v: Edwin V. Sumner
- competence above: McClellan's report: Sumner "moved promptly" to Glendale and sent two brigades to Franklin (OR-I-11-1-p67); McClellan's Fair Oaks dispatch credits his "great exertions" (OR-I-11-1-p751) [single judge]. Antietam not read.
- nerve above: led the cavalry at Cerro Gordo and Molino del Rey, wounded twice in the Seven Days (APPLETONS-sumner_edwin_v) [single source]; "fighting Generals" (HAY-D1-p53-54, initials expanded) [single source].
- experience high: 2d lieutenant 1819, brevet colonel (HEIT1-sumner_edwin_v).
- reputation_1861 above: brevets at Cerro Gordo and Molino del Rey for gallant and meritorious conduct (HEIT1-sumner_edwin_v), a public honour; brig gen USA 16 Mar 1861.

#### heintzelman_samuel_p: Samuel P. Heintzelman
- competence unknown: McClellan says Heintzelman and Kearny "most gallantly brought up their troops" at Fair Oaks (OR-I-11-1-p751): courage, not command judgement.
- nerve above: wounded at Bull Run, bvt for Fair Oaks (CULLUM-445; HEIT1-heintzelman_samuel_p); Hay's list (HAY-D1-p53-54, initials expanded) [single source].
- experience high: USMA 1826, 35 years' service, Mexican War brevet (HEIT1).
- reputation_1861 average: one gallantry brevet (Huamantla) (HEIT1-heintzelman_samuel_p), a public honour.

#### keyes_erasmus_d: Erasmus D. Keyes
- competence above: McClellan: "Great credit must be awarded to General Keyes for the skill and energy" of his covering duties (OR-I-11-1-p70) [single judge]; his memoir not read.
- nerve above: bvt for Fair Oaks (HEIT1-keyes_erasmus_d); Hay's list (HAY-D1-p53-54, initials expanded) [single source].
- experience above: Scott's aide 1837-41, military secretary 1860-61 (HEIT1; CULLUM-671).
- reputation_1861 unknown: no Mexican War brevet; office as Scott's military secretary is not a public honour.

#### porter_fitz_john: Fitz John Porter
- competence above: [contested]. McClellan: "energy and ability displayed by General Porter" at Hanover Court House (OR-I-11-1-p37). Brevet brigadier general for the Chickahominy; Director of the Siege of Yorktown (HEIT1-porter_fitz_john; CULLUM-1238). Stanton on 2 Sep 1862 said Pope had not been sustained by McClellan, Franklin and Porter (WELLES-D-1862-09-02) [single source].
- nerve above: two Mexican War brevets, Chickahominy brevet (HEIT1).
- experience above: USMA 1845, Mexican War, Utah expedition staff (CULLUM-1238).
- reputation_1861 above: two gallantry brevets (Molino del Rey, Chapultepec) (HEIT1-porter_fitz_john), a public honour.
- Sensitive: cashiered 21 Jan 1863; restored 1886 (HEIT1).

#### franklin_william_b: William B. Franklin
- competence above: McClellan's report calls the South Mountain action "brilliant", "conducted by General Franklin in all its details" (OR-I-19-1-p46) [single judge]; first in USMA class (HEIT1).
- nerve unknown: McClellan halted his advance at Annandale against Halleck's order (WELLES-D-1862-08-31); his own conduct not rated.
- experience above: USMA 1843 first, topographical engineers, Mexican War brevet (HEIT1-franklin_william_b).
- reputation_1861 average: one gallantry brevet (Buena Vista) (HEIT1-franklin_william_b), a public honour.

#### hooker_joseph: Joseph Hooker
- competence above: Heintzelman at Williamsburg: Hooker and his division "gallantly sustained themselves" (OR-I-11-1-p458); McClellan, 5 Aug 1862: "General Hooker's dispositions were admirable" (OR-I-11-1-p78); McClellan's Antietam report: his "services had been conspicuous and important" (OR-I-19-1-p30).
- nerve above: three Mexican War brevets (HEIT1-hooker_joseph); engaged at Williamsburg, Fair Oaks, Glendale (CULLUM-919).
- experience above: USMA 1837, Mexican War staff and line (HEIT1).
- reputation_1861 above: three gallantry brevets (Monterey, National Bridge, Chapultepec) (HEIT1-hooker_joseph), a public honour.
- drink_rumour: in the entry for 14 Jun 1863 Welles writes that Lincoln was the first to intimate it to him (WELLES-D-1863-06-14). A rumour, 1863.

#### meade_george_g: George G. Meade
- competence above: Hooker's Antietam report: "special mention of Brigadier General Meade for the great intelligence and gallantry displayed by him" (OR-I-19-1-p216); McCall: Meade came up and was "of most valuable service" (OR-I-11-2-p400). Welles of 28 Jun 1863 (outside window) says he knew little of him.
- nerve above: "gallantry" (Hooker, OR-I-19-1-p216); severely wounded at Glendale (CULLUM-804).
- experience above: USMA 1835, Mexican War brevet, captain 1856 (HEIT1-meade_george_g).
- reputation_1861 average: one gallantry brevet (Monterey) (HEIT1-meade_george_g), a public honour.

#### reynolds_john_f: John F. Reynolds
- competence above: McCall: Reynolds's study and preparations "justify his high reputation as a soldier"; his conduct of the right wing "worthy of all praise" (OR-I-11-2-p400) [single judge]. Captured at Glendale 30 Jun 1862 (CULLUM-1084).
- nerve above: gallantry brevets at Monterey and Buena Vista (HEIT1-reynolds_john_f) [proxy].
- experience above: USMA 1841, two brevets, captain 1855 (HEIT1).
- reputation_1861 above: two gallantry brevets (Monterey, Buena Vista) (HEIT1-reynolds_john_f), a public honour.

#### sigel_franz: Franz Sigel
- competence unknown: Appletons credits Pea Ridge and the right wing at Second Bull Run (APPLETONS-sigel_franz) [single source].
- nerve unknown: not rated.
- experience average: Baden military school and lieutenant; Heitman gives colonel 3d Missouri Infantry 4 May 1861 (HEIT1-sigel_franz). Details of 1848-49 role needs-source.
- reputation_1861 unknown: searched Appletons; no press evidence.

#### schurz_carl: Carl Schurz
- competence unknown; nerve unknown: no field evidence read (brigadier from 15 Apr 1862).
- experience low: no military service before 1862 (HEIT1-schurz_carl).
- reputation_1861 above: campaign speaker in English and German in 1860; appointed minister to Spain by Lincoln (APPLETONS-schurz_carl; BDAC-schurz_carl).

#### wadsworth_james_s: James S. Wadsworth
- competence unknown; nerve unknown: not rated.
- experience low: landowner; first military post volunteer aide, 8 Jul 1861 (HEIT1-wadsworth_james_s).
- reputation_1861 above: Republican elector 1856 and 1860, Peace Convention delegate 1861 (APPLETONS-wadsworth_james_s).
- antislavery: supported the Free-Soil party in 1848 and "continued to act in defence of the anti-slavery movement" (APPLETONS-wadsworth_james_s).

#### sickles_daniel_e: Daniel E. Sickles
- competence unknown: not rated.
- nerve above: "gained distinction" at Williamsburg, Fair Oaks, Malvern Hill (APPLETONS-sickles_daniel_e) [single source].
- experience low: militia major only (BDAC-sickles_daniel_e).
- reputation_1861 unknown: Congressman; 1859 killing of Key and acquittal (APPLETONS); press direction not searched.

#### hitchcock_ethan_a: Ethan A. Hitchcock
- competence unknown; nerve unknown: no 1862 evidence read; diary not read.
- experience high: USMA 1817, Scott's staff in Mexico, bvt brigadier general, colonel 2d Infantry (HEIT1-hitchcock_ethan_a; APPLETONS-hitchcock_ethan_a).
- reputation_1861 above: brevets of colonel and brigadier general for gallant and meritorious conduct at Contreras/Churubusco and Molino del Rey (HEIT1-hitchcock_ethan_a), a public honour; resigned 1855 after a leave dispute with the Secretary of War (APPLETONS).

#### Proposed tags

- none.

### Union generals: the West (`union_west`)

Page numbers for OR are the page headers of the civilwar.com transcription (not collated against print). Cullum pages are Thayer's transcription of the 3rd edition (1891).

#### grant_ulysses_s: Ulysses S. Grant
- competence high: as district commander took Forts Henry and Donelson, 14,623 prisoners (CULLUM-1187); Halleck, who had accused him, reported 15 Mar 1862 that Grant acted from good intentions and was told to resume command (OR-I-7-p683); at Shiloh ordered the dawn attack of 7 Apr, stating the advantage of becoming the attacking party (OR-I-10-1-p109) [own report]. Memoir adds the Donelson counter-attack decision and the surrender demand (GRANT-PM-ch22) [own memoir].
- nerve high: at Donelson, after the sortie, said whoever attacked first would win and ordered the assault at once (GRANT-PM-ch22) [own memoir, single source for the words]; Shiloh counter-attack as above (OR-I-10-1-p109).
- experience average: West Point 1843, Mexican War quartermaster with two brevets, captain 1853, resigned 1854, clerk and merchant at Galena 1860-61, largest command a company (CULLUM-1187, HEIT1-grant_ulysses_s).
- reputation_1861 low: a Galena merchant in March 1861 (CULLUM-1187); no contemporary notice read [single source].
- organiser: took a disorderly regiment and brought it to discipline in a few days (GRANT-PM-ch18) [own memoir].
- drink_rumour: Halleck wired McClellan on 4 Mar 1862 a rumour that Grant had resumed his former bad habits, and placed C. F. Smith in command (OR-I-7-p682); Halleck is the spreader; Halleck's own later report did not repeat it (OR-I-7-p683).
- insubordinate: went to Nashville after Donelson without Halleck's authority; Halleck called it mistaken zeal from good intentions (OR-I-7-p683, OR-I-7-p680 for McClellan's order to arrest). Grant says he never received Halleck's calls for returns and asked to be relieved (GRANT-PM-ch23) [own memoir]. [contested]
- harsh_with_civilians: General Orders No. 11, 17 Dec 1862, expelled "the Jews, as a class" from the department within 24 hours, by order of Grant (OR-I-17-2-p424).
- Also: after Shiloh Grant says he was "little more than an observer" and asked repeatedly to be relieved (GRANT-PM-ch26) [own memoir].

#### halleck_henry_w: Henry W. Halleck
- competence average: Halleck's 3 Mar 1862 dispatch to McClellan claims no returns from Grant and calls Smith "almost the only officer equal to the emergency" (OR-I-7-p680); on 15 Mar he reported Grant's trip was made with good intentions (OR-I-7-p683), i.e. he corrected an overreach. Welles, 10 Sep 1862, calls his intellect scholarly, his mind "heavy and irresolute", little real military talent (WELLES-D-1862-09-10) [single source, hostile]. McClellan thought Buell a far better soldier than Halleck (MCCLELLAN-OS-p243) [rival memoir: McClellan].
- nerve below: Welles "irresolute" (above); Grant describes the siege of Corinth as a methodical advance by intrenched lines (GRANT-PM-ch26) [rival memoir: Grant]; on 3-4 Mar 1862 he declined to arrest Grant at once ("not advisable at present") (OR-I-7-p682).
- experience average: West Point 3rd 1839, engineer, Mexican War staff in California, author of a treatise on military science 1846, lawyer and major-general of California militia 1860-61; no field command (CULLUM-988, HEIT1-halleck_henry_w).
- reputation_1861 above: author of Elements of Military Art and Science (1846, 2nd ed. 1858) and international-law treatise; re-appointed maj gen USA 19 Aug 1861 (CULLUM-988) [single source for the standing].
- slow / micromanages: Grant says orders went direct to Halleck's wings ignoring him and the advance moved by intrenched lines (GRANT-PM-ch26); Grant says Halleck called for troops and promised more (GRANT-PM-ch23) [rival memoir: Grant].
- makes_policy: General Orders No. 3, 20 Nov 1861, excluded fugitive slaves from camps and columns and expelled any inside, citing information reaching the enemy (OR-I-8-p370).

#### fremont_john_c: John C. Fremont
- competence below: Adjutant-General Thomas's inspection report of 21 Oct 1861 lists nearly 200 irregular appointments (OR-I-3-p541), staff contractors and contracts without bids (OR-I-3-p542, OR-I-3-p544) [hostile inspector; single source]. Lincoln's 11 Sep 1861 letter treats the proclamation as acceptable except the confiscation clause (OR-I-3-p485).
- nerve above: issued martial law and emancipation of rebels' slaves on his own decision overnight (OR-I-3-p467, p477) [own letter].
- experience average: Rocky Mountain and California expeditions (bvt capt), California Battalion, lt col mounted rifles, court-martialed 1847, US Senator 1850-51; no large army command (HEIT1-fremont_john_c, BDAC-fremont_john_c).
- reputation_1861 high: first Republican candidate for President, 1856 (BDAC-fremont_john_c; OCR reads 1866); commissioned maj gen 14 May 1861 (HEIT1-fremont_john_c).
- makes_policy / antislavery: proclamation of 30 Aug 1861: martial law in Missouri, property of rebels confiscated, "their slaves ... declared freemen" (OR-I-3-p467).
- secretive: wrote to Lincoln that he acted "without consultation or advise with any one" (OR-I-3-p477) [own letter]; Curtis told Thomas he never consulted subordinates and would not dare give opinions to him (OR-I-3-p541) [single source].
- insubordinate: a week after Lincoln's order modifying the proclamation he had 200 copies of the original printed for distribution (OR-I-3-p543) [hostile inspector, single source]. [contested]
- poor_administrator, contract_scandal: as above (OR-I-3-p541, p542); charge only.
- Removal: Scott's order of 24 Oct 1861 (OR-I-3-p553).

#### anderson_robert: Robert Anderson
- competence average: held Fort Sumter 34 hours, evacuated on the terms offered on 11 Apr (OR-I-1-p12) [own report]; in Kentucky relieved at his own request after strain (SHERMAN-M-ch9; OR-I-4-p296).
- nerve above: moved the garrison from Moultrie to Sumter on 26 Dec 1860 and sustained the bombardment (CULLUM-406; OR-I-1-p12).
- experience high: West Point 1825, Seminole and Mexican wars, assistant adjutant general, 35 years of service (CULLUM-406, HEIT1-anderson_robert).
- reputation_1861 above: the Moultrie move was a national controversy in Dec 1860 (NH-3-p56) [single source]; Northern press acclaim needs-source.
- infirm: Scott's telegram of 6 Oct 1861 ordering rest for restoration of health; Anderson relinquished the department 8 Oct (OR-I-4-p296, p297); retired 27 Oct 1863 for disability (CULLUM-406).

#### buell_don_c: Don Carlos Buell
- competence above: staff career, brevets at Monterey and Churubusco (HEIT1-buell_don_c); McClellan thought him "a far better soldier than Halleck" (MCCLELLAN-OS-p243) [rival memoir: McClellan]. Relieved 24 Oct 1862 (OR-I-16-2-p640).
- nerve below: declined the East Tennessee advance in Jan 1862 over Lincoln's stated anxiety (OR-I-7-p927, p928); government wished East Tennessee taken (OR-I-16-2-p640, to Rosecrans). McClellan defended him (MCCLELLAN-OS-p215). [contested]
- experience above: 20 years' service, Florida, Mexico, adjutant-general staff (CULLUM-1090).
- reputation_1861 average: staff officer, no public notice read; unknown beyond brevet and staff rank (CULLUM-1090) [single source].
- slow: Lincoln's letters of 6 and 13 Jan 1862 (OR-I-7-p927-928). [contested]

#### sherman_william_t: William T. Sherman
- competence above: Shiloh: Grant praises his judgement and skill and that he stayed through two days though wounded (OR-I-10-1-p110) [own commander's report]; Kentucky 1861 was marred by reports of 200,000 needed (OR-I-4-p314).
- nerve above: wounded in the hand at Shiloh, three horses killed, held his place (OR-I-10-1-p110) [commander's report]; his own memoir records the published charge that he was insane over the 200,000 men (SHERMAN-M-ch9) [own memoir].
- experience average: West Point 6th 1840, California staff in the Mexican War, resigned 1853, banker and seminary superintendent (CULLUM-1022).
- reputation_1861 low: civilian in March 1861 (CULLUM-1022) [single source].
- overestimates_enemy: told Cameron 200,000 men needed in Kentucky; Cameron judged he overestimated the rebels (OR-I-4-p314).
- self_doubting: says he renewed his wish to stay in a subordinate position (SHERMAN-M-ch9) [own memoir].

#### rosecrans_william_s: William S. Rosecrans
- competence above: Rich Mountain, Carnifex Ferry, command of an army at Iuka and Corinth, named to relieve Buell (CULLUM-1115; OR-I-16-2-p640); Grant says he held a high opinion of him at the time (GRANT-PM-ch28) [rival memoir: Grant].
- nerve above: fought at Iuka and held Corinth, 3-4 Oct 1862 (CULLUM-1115) [single source; OR reports not read].
- experience average: West Point 5th 1842, engineer, resigned 1854, civil engineer and oil manufacturer (CULLUM-1115).
- reputation_1861 low: civilian in March 1861 (CULLUM-1115) [single source].
- slow: Grant says Rosecrans put no troops on the Fulton road at Iuka and did not pursue promptly after Corinth (GRANT-PM-ch28, GRANT-PM-ch29) [rival memoir: Grant].
- organiser: organized and equipped Ohio Home Guards and was chief engineer of Ohio (CULLUM-1115).

#### lyon_nathaniel: Nathaniel Lyon
- competence above: defended the arsenal and broke up Camp Jackson on 10 May 1861 (CULLUM-1069); Congress thanked his troops (HEIT1-lyon_nathaniel).
- nerve high: held at Springfield with an embarrassed position, "I shall hold my ground as long as possible" (OR-I-3-p57); killed at Wilsons Creek.
- experience average: West Point 11th 1841, Florida and Mexico, captain 1851 (CULLUM-1069).
- reputation_1861 low: captain on the frontier until Feb 1861 (CULLUM-1069) [single source].
- candid: 9 Aug 1861 letter to Frémont (OR-I-3-p57).

#### thomas_george_h: George H. Thomas
- competence high: Mill Springs victory, report 31 Jan 1862 (OR-I-7-p79); thanks of Congress 1865 (HEIT1-thomas_george_h).
- nerve above: rode forward at Mill Springs to see the enemy's position (OR-I-7-p80); thanks of Congress for "dauntless courage" (HEIT1-thomas_george_h).
- experience above: West Point 1840, Florida, Mexico (three brevets), major 2nd Cavalry (CULLUM-1028).
- reputation_1861 average: major of cavalry; Virginia birth; stayed with the Union (CULLUM-1028) [single source].
- Logan's Cross Roads: halted his column for rations on 2 Jan 1862 (OR-I-7-p927). Not tagged.

#### curtis_samuel_r: Samuel R. Curtis
- competence above: Pea Ridge, a winter campaign of over 1,000 miles to Helena (CULLUM-655; OR-I-8-p196).
- nerve above: won at Pea Ridge, 7-8 Mar 1862, against a force he put at 30,000-40,000 (OR-I-8-p197) [own report].
- experience average: West Point 1831, Mexican War colonel holding Camargo, chief engineer, Congressman (CULLUM-655, BDAC-curtis_samuel_r).
- reputation_1861 above: Republican Representative 1857-61, peace-convention member (BDAC-curtis_samuel_r).
- candid: told Thomas he thought Frémont unequal to command (OR-I-3-p541) [single source].

#### smith_charles_f: Charles F. Smith
- competence high: Halleck: "almost the only officer equal to the emergency" (OR-I-7-p680); Cullum cites Donelson, Scott's choice for Department of Washington (CULLUM-410).
- nerve high: on 15 Feb 1862 Grant ordered Smith to charge the outworks and Smith went in advance of his men to keep them from firing in the abatis (GRANT-PM-ch22) [own memoir, single source for the act]; Grant adds that Lincoln's view was that the victory was due to Smith (GRANT-PM-ch23), reported through Halleck's staff [own memoir].
- experience high: West Point 1825, commandant of cadets, three Mexican War brevets, commander Department of Utah (CULLUM-410).
- reputation_1861 above: Scott called him to command the Department of Washington, April 1861 (CULLUM-410) [single source, after March].
- infirm: injured and sick before Shiloh, died 25 Apr 1862 (CULLUM-410).

#### mcclernand_john_a: John A. McClernand
- competence average: Grant names him with the other division commanders who "maintained their places with credit" (OR-I-10-1-p110); Grant doubted his fitness (GRANT-PM-ch30) [rival memoir: Grant].
- nerve above: fought at Donelson and Shiloh (OR-I-10-1-p110) [single source].
- experience low: private in 1832; state legislator; Representative (HEIT1-mcclernand_john_a; BDAC-mcclernand_john_a).
- reputation_1861 above: Democratic Representative who took strong Union ground, praised by Republican papers (GRANT-PM-ch18) [own memoir of another; BDAC for office].
- ambitious / political_patron: authorized from Washington to organize troops; authority from the President and Secretary of War (GRANT-PM-ch29, GRANT-PM-ch30) [rival memoir: Grant].

#### wallace_lewis: Lew Wallace
- competence average: Grant's dispatch says his division was led by a circuitous route and missed Sunday's action at Shiloh (OR-I-10-1-p109); named with credit (OR-I-10-1-p110) [single source].
- nerve unknown: searched Grant memoir, Heitman, OR p109-110; no act read.
- experience below: Mexican War second lieutenant 1846-47, colonel 1861 (HEIT1-wallace_lewis).
- reputation_1861 unknown: no source read.

#### mitchel_ormsby_m: Ormsby M. Mitchel
- competence above: organizing volunteers, taking Bowling Green and Nashville positions, securing the north Alabama railroad (CULLUM-555) [single source].
- nerve above: Cullum calls him bold in lecturing; field boldness read only in his sketch (CULLUM-555) [single source].
- experience below: resigned 1832; professor, astronomer, adjutant general of Ohio (CULLUM-555).
- reputation_1861 above: fifteen years as a popular lecturer, director of observatories (CULLUM-555) [single source].
- organiser: organizing volunteers at Louisville, Nov-Dec 1861 (CULLUM-555).

#### nelson_william: William Nelson
- competence unknown, nerve unknown, reputation_1861 unknown: searched Heitman, Sherman and Grant memoirs, Welles; only commission facts and the Shiloh advance (OR-I-10-1-p109) found.
- experience below: naval lieutenant; no army command (HEIT1-nelson_william); Sherman records him a navy officer commissioned brigadier general at Camp Dick Robinson (SHERMAN-M-ch9).

#### schofield_john_m: John M. Schofield
- competence above: Wilsons Creek staff work, acting adjutant general report (OR-I-3-p57); command of Missouri militia (CULLUM-1585) [single source].
- nerve above: Medal of Honor, 1892, for leading a regiment in a successful charge at Wilsons Creek (HEIT1-schofield_john_m).
- experience below: West Point 7th 1853, lieutenant, professor (CULLUM-1585).
- reputation_1861 low: lieutenant on leave, physics professor (CULLUM-1585) [single source].

#### hurlbut_stephen_a: Stephen A. Hurlbut
- competence average: Grant's dispatch lists Hurlbut with the division commanders who held their places with credit (OR-I-10-1-p110); promoted maj gen 17 Sep 1862 (HEIT1-hurlbut_stephen_a) [single source].
- nerve average: Shiloh division, the first to reach Pittsburg Landing (APPLETONS-hurlbut_stephen_a) [single source].
- experience below: Florida War adjutant, lawyer, state legislator (BDAC-hurlbut_stephen_a).
- reputation_1861 low: Illinois legislator; personal friend of Lincoln (NH-3-p390).
- candid: report of 27 Mar 1861: no attachment to the Union in Charleston, separate nationality a fixed fact (NH-3-p391).

### Naval officers, Union and Confederate (`navies`)

Flags: [single source], [own memoir], [rival memoir: author], [contested]. Welles's diary is Welles's view. Keys are defined in navies-sources.md.

#### farragut_david_g: David G. Farragut
- competence high: orders of 20 Jan 1862 gave him the New Orleans expedition; he passed Forts Jackson and St. Philip with his fleet and took the city, and Congress thanked him (CALLAHAN-THANKS, approved 11 Jul 1862; BL-2-p28-29; BL-2-p41-45). Welles, 10 Aug 1862, did not blame the flag officers for Vicksburg but thought Farragut should have gone up river at once after New Orleans; on 20 Aug he called both flag officers delinquent over the ram Arkansas (WELLES-D-1862-08-10; WELLES-D-1862-08-20) [Welles's view; contested]
- nerve high: Porter describes him walking the Hartford's poop calmly while she burned off the forts (BL-2-p41-45) [participant memoir: Porter, who commanded the mortar flotilla and later quarrelled over credit; not independent]; on 28 Jun 1862 he ran the Vicksburg batteries with eight vessels (APPLETONS-farragut_david_g) [single source for the Vicksburg run]
- experience high: midshipman 17 Dec 1810, captain 14 Sep 1855 (CALLAHAN-farragut_david_g); founded the Mare Island yard 1854-58 and commanded the sloop Saratoga in the Mexican War (APPLETONS-farragut_david_g); no flag command before 1862
- reputation_1861 unknown: searched Appletons, Welles's diary and Callahan; found only that he spent about a year on a Brooklyn retiring board after leaving Norfolk on 18 Apr 1861 (APPLETONS-farragut_david_g); no March 1861 press or honour located
- dutiful: wanted to take his fleet to Mobile but kept the ships in the river as the government wished (APPLETONS-farragut_david_g) [single source]
- popular_with_troops: a shipmate's letter praises his discipline of the Natchez crew, 'contented and happy' (APPLETONS-farragut_david_g) [single source; quoted by Appletons, 1830s]

#### du_pont_samuel_f: Samuel F. Du Pont
- competence above: took Port Royal on 7 Nov 1861 and Congress thanked him (CALLAHAN-THANKS, approved 22 Feb 1862); as president of the officers' conference he produced the memoirs that became the blockade plan, which Scott adopted (BL-1-p671-674) [participant memoir: Ammen served under him and used his private letters; Du Pont's own letter-extracts, not independently checked]. Welles, 2 Oct 1862: 'a skillful and accomplished officer' (WELLES-D-1862-10-02) [Welles's view]
- nerve below: Welles, 16 Feb 1863, feared Du Pont 'shrinks, dreads the conflict he has sought'; 12 Mar 1863, 'as prudent as McClellan'; 2 Apr 1863 he recorded Lincoln likening Du Pont's calls for more ironclads to McClellan's for regiments (WELLES-D-1863-02-16; WELLES-D-1863-03-12; WELLES-D-1863-04-02) [Welles's view; contested: he led the squadron into Port Royal under fire]
- experience high: midshipman 19 Dec 1815, commander 28 Oct 1842, captain 14 Sep 1855 (CALLAHAN-du_pont_samuel_f); Mexican War operations on the California coast, China 1857-59, Philadelphia Navy Yard from 31 Dec 1860 (APPLETONS-du_pont_samuel_f); had not commanded so large a fleet before 1861 [single source]
- reputation_1861 above: captain with Mexican War and China service, named president of the blockade conference in June 1861 (APPLETONS-du_pont_samuel_f) [single source; the June appointment is after the March date]
- organiser: conference memoirs of June-Sept 1861 and the Port Royal expedition fitted in New York (BL-1-p671-674)
- joint_minded: Sherman was ordered to organise the 12,000-man expedition 'in connection with Captain Du Pont' (BL-1-p673) [participant memoir: Ammen]
- slow: Welles, 12 Mar and 2 Apr 1863 (WELLES-D-1863-03-12; WELLES-D-1863-04-02) [Welles's view; the dated evidence falls in 1863]
- Welles also notes (10 Aug 1862) that he was 'watchful but slow to express apprehension' about the Fingal (WELLES-D-1862-08-10), and on 2 Oct 1862 that he was 'a courtier ... given to cliques' (WELLES-D-1862-10-02); no tag fits cleanly

#### foote_andrew_h: Andrew H. Foote
- competence above: took Fort Henry on 6 Feb 1862 with the gunboats and fought Donelson on 14 Feb, where the boats were disabled; Congress thanked him on 19 Mar and 16 Jul 1862 (CALLAHAN-THANKS; APPLETONS-foote_andrew_h; BL-1-p434). Welles, 10 Oct 1862: 'performed wonders and dissipated many prejudices' (WELLES-D-1862-10-10) [Welles's view]
- nerve high: at Donelson he took the boats to about 500 yards of the batteries and was 'not content' (BL-1-p413; article 'The Capture of Fort Donelson', author not verified from the OCR); Walke puts the St. Louis at about 350 yards when Foote was wounded (BL-1-p434) [participant memoir: Walke, his subordinate]
- experience above: midshipman 4 Dec 1822, lieutenant 27 May 1830, commander 19 Dec 1852 (CALLAHAN-foote_andrew_h); commanded the Perry on the African coast 1849-51 and took the Canton barrier forts in 1856 (APPLETONS-foote_andrew_h); first large command in 1861
- reputation_1861 above: the 1856 storming of the barrier forts is the public action in his record (APPLETONS-foote_andrew_h) [single source; no March 1861 press found]
- infirm: wounded at Donelson; on crutches when he reported to the Bureau in Aug 1862; stricken in 1863 on the way to command (WELLES-D-1862-08-10; BL-1-p346) [Eads is a friend and partisan]
- organiser: 'equipment and organization of this flotilla taxed the energies' and he called it his greatest work (APPLETONS-foote_andrew_h); the thanks of 19 Mar 1862 cites opening the rivers (CALLAHAN-THANKS)
- Welles (25 and 27 Aug 1862) records a petulant letter about clerks followed by a handsome apology (WELLES-D-1862-08-25; WELLES-D-1862-08-27); not tagged

#### goldsborough_louis_m: Louis M. Goldsborough
- competence average: Congress thanked him for Roanoke Island (CALLAHAN-THANKS, 11 Jul 1862); Soley says concentrating the North Atlantic, sounds and Wilmington duties in one flag officer at a distance had injurious results (BL-2-p264) [Soley, a naval academy professor writing later]; Welles, 10 Aug 1862, judged him 'inefficient ... done nothing effective since the frigates were sunk' (WELLES-D-1862-08-10) [Welles's view]
- nerve average: Barnard's letters to McClellan (19-20 Mar 1862) report him confident he could keep down the Merrimac, opposed to the Sewell's Point plan, and unwilling to spare more than a list of small vessels (MCCLELLAN-OS-p246-247) [own memoir: McClellan prints Barnard; interested parties]; Soley says he offered McClellan the squadron's cooperation against Fort Darling if the army attacked (BL-2-p268-270) [contested; Soley cites Goldsborough's own testimony]
- experience above: midshipman 18 Jun 1812, lieutenant 13 Jan 1825, commander 8 Sep 1841, captain 14 Sep 1855 (CALLAHAN-goldsborough_louis_m); war service and shore commands: needs-source
- reputation_1861 unknown: no contemporary judgement found for March 1861
- rank_proud: Welles says Wilkes would not report to him and, when Wilkes was given an independent James River command, Goldsborough 'took offense, and asked to resign' (WELLES-D-1862-08-10) [Welles's view; single source]
- Burnside's orders of 7 Jan 1862 show him already at Fort Monroe as flag officer (MCCLELLAN-OS-p207)

#### porter_david_d: David D. Porter
- competence above: Welles, 1 Oct 1862, picked him for the Western Flotilla: 'stirring and positive qualities, fertile in resources, great energy', but 'not considered by some of our best naval men a fortunate officer'; on 10 Oct 'reckless, improvident' (WELLES-D-1862-10-01; WELLES-D-1862-10-10) [Welles's view]. His mortar flotilla fired for six days at the forts without reducing them (BL-2-p32, editors' note; Porter's own account BL-2-p24) [own memoir]. Thanks of Congress for Arkansas Post, Jan 1863 (CALLAHAN-THANKS, 7 Feb 1863; outside the window)
- nerve high: 'brave and daring like all his family' (WELLES-D-1862-10-01) [Welles's view; single source]
- experience above: midshipman (Callahan 2 Feb 1829), lieutenant 27 Feb 1841, commander only 22 Apr 1861 (CALLAHAN-porter_david_d); Mexican War, coast survey, mail steamers (APPLETONS-porter_david_d); no squadron command before 1862
