- experience average: Representative 1845-47 and 1849-51, peace-convention member, Provisional Congress delegate (BDAC-seddon_james_a).
- reputation_1861 above: Jones calls him the "so-called Demosthenean Seddon" among the speakers at the Virginia convention (JONES-I-1861-04-16); BDAC shows peace-convention membership [single source].
- Jones notes he took his seat on 21 Nov 1862 (JONES-I-1862-11-21) and that Davis frowns on military bodies outside his own control, an awkward position for the new Secretary (JONES-I-1862-11-29); the contract charge of 22 Dec 1862 is a diary charge only (JONES-I-1862-12-22).

#### memminger_christopher_g: Christopher G. Memminger
- competence below [contested]: Jones says he pays a 50 per cent premium in notes for specie and asks "what sort of financiering" (JONES-I-1862-01-20); Tyler, per Jones, called his head "as worthless as a pin's-head" (JONES-I-1862-12-12) [clerk's view]; Davis (own memoir) says he "proved himself entirely worthy" (BL-1-p106).
- nerve unknown: nothing read.
- experience above: chaired Ways and Means in the South Carolina House (BL-1-p104) [rival memoir: Rhett Jr., single source].
- reputation_1861 above: Davis says "high reputation for knowledge of finance" and integrity (BL-1-p106) [own memoir]; Rhett says industrious and shrewd (BL-1-p104).

#### mallory_stephen_r: Stephen R. Mallory
- competence above [contested]: his letter of 8 May 1861 to Conrad argued an ironclad was "a matter of the first necessity" (BL-1-p631, Soley); Soley says he acted with "vigor and intelligence" (BL-1-p630). Rhett: "feeble and dilatory" (BL-1-p105); Kennon: the Louisiana was ordered only four months before New Orleans fell (BL-2-p76) [rival memoirs].
- nerve above [single source]: Soley credits prompt and bold action on ironclads (BL-1-p631).
- experience above: Senator 1851-61 and chairman of Naval Affairs (BDAC-mallory_stephen_r; BL-1-p106).
- reputation_1861 above: Davis chose him for his Naval Committee record (BL-1-p106) [own memoir]; Jones heard him "soundly abused" in May 1861 for refusing ironclad plans (JONES-I-1861-05-26).
- slow: Rhett (BL-1-p105) and Kennon (BL-2-p76) [rival memoirs].

#### toombs_robert_a: Robert Toombs
- competence unknown: five months at State; Rhett and Davis rate his mind (BL-1-p104, BL-1-p106) but no work read.
- nerve high: told the cabinet 500,000 volunteers should be taken for the war and said he would have taken responsibility in Davis's place (JONES-I-1861-05-22); urged taking Fort Pickens at a cost of a thousand lives (JONES-I-1861-05-23); Chesnut calls him a "bold brigadier" charging recklessly (CHESNUT-1905-p97).
- experience high: House 1845-53, Senate 1853-61, Georgia legislature, Creek War captain (BDAC-toombs_robert_a).
- reputation_1861 high: Rhett says he got State by public appreciation of his "great mental powers" (BL-1-p104); Davis offered him Treasury first (BL-1-p106).
- quarrelsome: Brewster, via Chesnut, says he left the cabinet from "incompatibility of temper" with Davis (CHESNUT-1905-p108) [single source, hearsay].

#### hunter_robert_m_t: Robert M. T. Hunter
- competence unknown, nerve unknown: searched Jones, Chesnut, Battles and Leaders. Jones thought Benjamin outranked him and he could not stay (JONES-I-1861-10-14) but gives no act.
- experience high: Speaker of the House, Senator 1847-61 (BDAC-hunter_robert_m_t).
- reputation_1861 high: candidate for the 1860 presidential nomination (BDAC-hunter_robert_m_t) [single source].

#### reagan_john_h: John H. Reagan
- competence unknown, nerve unknown: no postal report read. Jones reports a sharp letter to the War Secretary in Dec 1862 (JONES-I-1862-12-09).
- experience above: district judge, legislator, Representative 1857-61 (BDAC-reagan_john_h).
- reputation_1861 average: Davis called him a "sturdy, honest Representative" (BL-1-p106) [own memoir, single source].

#### stephens_alexander_h: Alexander H. Stephens
- competence unknown: no act as Vice President read in 1861-62.
- nerve above [single source]: his letter, published 3 Oct 1862, denied any power to declare martial law (JONES-I-1862-10-03); the Chesnut editors call him courageous (CHESNUT-1905-p12).
- experience high: House 1843-59 (BDAC-stephens_alexander_h).
- reputation_1861 high: chosen Vice President by the Provisional Congress (BDAC-stephens_alexander_h); Jones expected Davis and Stephens unopposed (JONES-I-1861-10-29).
- opposes_emancipation: Cornerstone speech, 21 Mar 1861: slavery was "the immediate cause of the late rupture"; the new government's corner-stone is "that the negro is not equal to the white man; that slavery... is his natural and normal condition" (STEPHENS-1861-03-21-CORNERSTONE).
- Rhett says his plan to buy cotton for bonds was opposed by the administration (BL-1-p110).

#### cooper_samuel: Samuel Cooper
- competence average: Jones complains of his office (JONES-I-1862-06-24, JONES-I-1862-11-02, JONES-I-1862-12-04) partly on grounds of his Northern birth; his 25 Apr 1861 report asked for two more infantry regiments and a cavalry regiment (OR-IV-1-p253).
- nerve unknown: nothing read.
- experience high: Adjutant General USA 1852-61, brevet colonel 1848 (CULLUM-156, HEIT1-cooper_samuel).
- reputation_1861 high: first of the full generals (WRIGHT-p9); Jones calls him first on the list of regular generals (JONES-I-1861-05-26).

#### gorgas_josiah: Josiah Gorgas
- competence unknown: assigned chief of Ordnance on 8 Apr 1861 (OR-IV-1-p211); acting-chief estimates in Walker's report (OR-IV-1-p248). No judgement of his work read.
- nerve unknown.
- experience above: twenty years in Ordnance, four arsenal commands (CULLUM-1064).
- reputation_1861 unknown: only a class rank of 6 (CULLUM-1064).

#### northrop_lucius_b: Lucius B. Northrop
- competence below [contested]: Imboden says a board found he failed to send supplies and blocked collection near Manassas (BL-1-p239) [rival memoir: Imboden]; Chesnut calls him the most abused man in the Confederacy and "may not be efficient" (CHESNUT-1905-p97); Jones reports a board of inquiry on his contracts and his claim that Virginia had too little wheat (JONES-I-1862-10-30, JONES-I-1862-11-06). Northrop denies it and cites a joint committee (BL-1-p261) [own memoir].
- nerve unknown.
- experience below: dragoon line officer 1831-39, then on sick leave and dropped in 1848, doctor from 1853 (CULLUM-650, HEIT1-northrop_lucius_b); no subsistence service shown.
- reputation_1861 unknown: obscure in March 1861; Chesnut shows him vilified by July (CHESNUT-1905-p97).
- political_patron: Davis served with him in the 1st Dragoons 1833-35 (CULLUM-530, CULLUM-650) and Jones calls him a "special favorite" (JONES-I-1861-10-15).
- contract_scandal (charge): JONES-I-1862-10-30. shifts_blame: BL-1-p261.

#### wigfall_louis_t: Louis T. Wigfall
- competence above [single source]: Chesnut calls him the worst enemy Davis had in Congress (CHESNUT-1905-p101); chaired Territories and reported the habeas bill (JC-II-p20, JC-II-p26).
- nerve high: rowed to Sumter under fire on 13 Apr 1861 and demanded surrender (BL-1-p73, BL-1-p83); Chesnut p42.
- experience average: Senator 1859-61, Texas legislator, Seminole War lieutenant (BDAC-wigfall_louis_t).
- reputation_1861 above: Chesnut's editors call him an uncompromising defender of the South on slavery (CHESNUT-1905-p29); "stormy petrel" (CHESNUT-1905-p33).
- insubordinate: Chisolm says he acted on assumed authority (BL-1-p83). disdains_superiors: spoke with his hat on while Davis spoke bareheaded (CHESNUT-1905-p96) [single source]. quarrelsome: CHESNUT-1905-p48, CHESNUT-1905-p101.

#### cobb_howell: Howell Cobb
- competence unknown, nerve unknown: elected permanent President of the Provisional Congress by acclamation (JC-I-p16); no further act read.
- experience high: Speaker, Governor, Treasury Secretary (BDAC-cobb_howell).
- reputation_1861 high: put forward by Georgia for President (BL-1-p101); Chesnut says some preferred him (CHESNUT-1905-p6).

### Confederate generals: the East (`confederate_east`)

Directions are for the true-stat centre, with cited acts or contemporaries' judgements. Flags: [own memoir], [rival memoir: author], [single source], [contested], [own report]. Page cites are to OR page ranges read from the running headers of the scans; where a passage sits between two detected headers the range is given.

#### lee_robert_e: Robert E. Lee
- competence high: Seven Days plan as he reported it: held Richmond's lines with Huger and Magruder while Jackson and the others swept the north bank, and Jackson's Valley campaign had kept McDowell from McClellan (OR-I-11-2-p489-498, p490) [own report]. Davis's endorsement on the Magruder papers treats Lee's account as the one that will "give a different aspect" (OR-I-11-2-p679-680). Jackson's remark that Lee was "cautious" but "not slow" is repeated by Lee's son (LEE-1904-p94-95) [partisan compilation, secondhand, single source]. [contested]: the 1861 western Virginia plan failed at Cheat Mountain (OR-I-5-p192-193) and the Seven Days were won at heavy cost.
- nerve high: sent Jackson, Longstreet and both Hills north of the Chickahominy and ordered Huger and Magruder only to hold their positions (OR-I-11-2-p489-498, p490) [own report].
- experience high: Engineers from USMA 1829 (2nd in class), staff officer in Mexico with three brevets to bvt col, USMA Superintendent 1852-55, led the Harpers Ferry force 1859 and the Dept of Texas 1860 (CULLUM-542, HEIT1-lee_robert_e).
- reputation_1861 high: three Mexican War brevets and col 1 Cav from 16 Mar 1861 (HEIT1-lee_robert_e, CULLUM-542) [single source, register only]; no press or contemporary quotation found.
- posts: accepted command of the Virginia forces before the state convention as Colonel Lee (LEE-1904-p350-351) [partisan compilation, single source; date needs-source]; signing as General, Commanding at Huntersville on 5 Aug 1861 (OR-I-5-p770) and from Meadow Bluff on 21 Sep (OR-I-5-p868); in command of the coast department 9 Nov 1861 (OR-I-6-p312-313); called to Richmond 3 Mar and charged with operations 13 Mar 1862 (OR-I-6-p2-3); took the Army of Northern Virginia on 1 Jun 1862 (LEE-1904-p72-73) [single source].
- (continued posts) ANV general orders headed Army of Northern Virginia were issued by 24 Jun 1862 (OR-I-11-2-p498-501).
- candid: told Wise on 21 Sep 1861 the forces were not united and it "would be the height of imprudence" to leave them apart (OR-I-5-p868); on 14 Aug 1862 told the Secretary of War that Magruder's difficulties "could not have been more readily overcome" while crediting his effort (OR-I-11-2-p679-680).
- slaveholder: executor of the Custis estate, whose will freed its enslaved people after a term; he signed manumission deeds in Dec 1862 and wrote of those hired out in Richmond and "carried away" (LEE-1904-p89-90) [partisan compilation by his son, single source]. Personal ownership in census schedules: needs-source.
- Rank conflict: Johnston calls Lee a lt col in the Aug 1861 ranking (JOHNSTON-1874-p71-73) [rival memoir: Johnston]; registers give col 1 Cav from 1861-03-16 (HEIT1-lee_robert_e, CULLUM-542).

#### johnston_joseph_e: Joseph E. Johnston
- competence above: Grant, as a rival, called him "one of the ablest commanders of the South" (GRANT-PM-ch62) [rival memoir: Grant]. His Seven Pines plan miscarried partly on confused orders and Huger blamed him (OR-I-11-1-p936-938) [contested]; he praised Longstreet and D. H. Hill by name (OR-I-11-1-p933-935).
- nerve average: ordered the 31 May 1862 attack (OR-I-11-1-p933-935) but judged the Yorktown line untenable and withdrew (OR-I-11-1-p275-276) [own report].
- experience high: USMA 1829, two decades in Florida, Mexico (wounded at Cerro Gordo) and staff, Acting Inspector General in Utah 1858, brig gen as Quartermaster General from 28 Jun 1860 (CULLUM-553, HEIT1-johnston_joseph_e).
- reputation_1861 high: three brevets and the brig gen staff grade (HEIT1-johnston_joseph_e) [single source, register only].
- rank_proud: Aug 1861 letter to Davis protesting that the list put him fourth; he describes the ranking as "altogether illegal" (JOHNSTON-1874-p71-73) [own memoir; letter itself and Davis reply of 14 Sep not found in the OR read].
- shifts_blame: his Seven Pines report said Keyes' corps would have been destroyed had Huger's division been up; Huger answered that Johnston never told him Longstreet commanded and "shields himself" (OR-I-11-1-p933-935; p936-938) [rival: Huger; contested].
- secretive: Lee wrote on 5 Mar 1862 "Not knowing your position or movements" and the President awaited a reply to his 1 Mar dispatch (OR-I-5-p1090) [single source].
- candid: 22 Apr 1862 told Lee the Yorktown labour was "wretchedly misapplied" and that "no one but McClellan could have hesitated to attack" (OR-I-11-3-p455-456); wrote that the loss of a division would leave the line too weak (OR-I-11-3-p419-420).
- posts: Davis's orders show him commanding Department of Northern Virginia by 21 Dec 1861 (OR-I-5-p1012); on 6 Apr 1862 he told Lee he could not decide from the Rapidan whether troops were less needed there, since "He who directs military operations upon information from every department can" (OR-I-11-3-p423).
- infirm: struck from his horse about sunset 31 May 1862, severely wounded; "prevented by feebleness" from reporting sooner (OR-I-11-1-p934-935); back in command only in Nov 1862 (date needs-source).

#### beauregard_pierre_g_t: Pierre G. T. Beauregard
- competence above: reduced Fort Sumter after completing channel defences and batteries (OR-I-1-p30-32) [own report]; Cullum records fortification work 1838-60 (CULLUM-942). Shiloh: reported a "complete victory" on 6 Apr (OR-I-10-1-p384-385) [own report] and the army withdrew; [contested]. Manassas report (OR vol 2, p484) not read.
- nerve unknown: searched his Sumter report (OR-I-1-p30-32) and Shiloh telegram (OR-I-10-1-p384-385); both are own-report claims and neither shows a risk taken or avoided. The Tupelo leave of June 1862 and Shiloh orders were not read (needs-source).
- experience average: engineer captain, two brevets, wounded twice at Mexico City, USMA Superintendent five days in Jan 1861 (CULLUM-942, HEIT1-beauregard_pierre_g_t); no command above a work party before 1861.
- reputation_1861 above: two Mexican War brevets (HEIT1-beauregard_pierre_g_t) [single source, register only]; first appearance Charleston, Mar 1861.
- boastful: telegram of 6 Apr 1862 "gained a complete victory", and the detailed report speaks of "all the substantial fruits of a complete victory" (OR-I-10-1-p384-385) before the Federal reinforcement of 7 Apr.
- Not tagged: the 5 Jun 1861 proclamation to Loudoun, Fairfax and Prince William accusing Lincoln's troops of the war-cry "Beauty and booty" (OR-I-2-p907) is inflammatory rhetoric about the enemy rather than a boast; proposed tag: `inflammatory_rhetoric` (not in vocabulary).
- posts: transferred the Charleston harbor forces to Col R. H. Anderson in May 1861 (OR-I-6-p1, footnote); relieved at Centreville by Johnston's order of 30 Jan 1862 for another department (OR-I-5-p1052-1053); succeeded A. S. Johnston at Shiloh and kept command through the retreat to Corinth (GRANT-PM-ch25) [rival memoir: Grant]; still signing as commanding the Western Department at Tupelo on 9 Jun 1862 (OR-I-10-2-p601-602).
- Tupelo leave, Davis's relief, Charleston 1862: needs-source.

#### jackson_thomas_j: Thomas J. Jackson
- competence high: Johnston, forwarding on 7 Feb 1862 Jackson's request to leave the army, wrote "I don't know how the loss of this officer can be supplied" (OR-I-5-p1053); Lee reported that Jackson's Valley command had diverted McDowell from McClellan (OR-I-11-2-p490). Valley report itself not read.
- nerve high: attacked Shields at Kernstown on 23 Mar 1862 with a smaller force (OR-I-12-1-p379-380); Johnston noted he "attacked the enemy under a misapprehension as to his force" [contested as judgement; the act shows willingness to fight].
- experience average: USMA 1846 (17th), Mexican War with three brevets to bvt maj, resigned 1852, ten years as a professor; never held a field command before 1861 (CULLUM-1288, HEIT1-jackson_thomas_j).
- reputation_1861 average: register brevets only (HEIT1-jackson_thomas_j) [single source]; no press or contemporary opinion found. Held no rank in Mar 1861.
- candid: his 24 Mar 1862 report said the enemy "repulsed me" with loss of valuable officers and a gun (OR-I-12-1-p379-380).
- Romney: on 31 Jan 1862 he wrote that "with such interference in my command I cannot expect to be of much service in the field" and asked for VMI or resignation; the order he cites withdrew Loring (OR-I-5-p1053). The petition by Loring's officers and Benjamin's order text: needs-source.
- slaveholder: not recorded; needs-source (census slave schedule or 1892 life by his widow).

#### longstreet_james: James Longstreet
- competence above: Johnston wrote that Longstreet's "clear head and brave heart" left him no reason to interfere at Williamsburg (OR-I-11-1-p275-276) and that "skill, vigor, and decision" at Seven Pines "are worthy of the highest praise" (OR-I-11-1-p933-935) [single source: Johnston]. Huger disputes Seven Pines orders (OR-I-11-1-p936-938) [rival].
- nerve above: "brave heart", led the right wing at Seven Pines (OR-I-11-1-p275-276; p933-935) [single source].
- experience average: USMA 1842, Mexico (wounded at Chapultepec, two brevets), adjutant, then staff paymaster from July 1858; largest command a company (CULLUM-1164, HEIT1-longstreet_james).
- reputation_1861 average: two brevets (HEIT1-longstreet_james) [single source, register only].
- Searched and not found: `slow` (Second Manassas needs OR vol 12 Confederate reports, not read); his own memoir not used for claims about others.

#### magruder_john_b: John B. Magruder
- competence average: Johnston praised "the line of little works constructed by Major-General Magruder's forethought" at Williamsburg (OR-I-11-1-p275-276); Lee answered seven points of his Seven Days report (OR-I-11-2-p679-680) and regretted that Magruder's difficulties "could not have been more readily overcome" while assuring the Secretary he "intentionally omitted nothing" (same pages); Lee's report says he did not reach Holmes in time on 30 Jun (OR-I-11-2-p495-496). Longstreet says he insisted on his own guides at Malvern Hill (LONGSTREET-1896-ch11) [rival memoir: Longstreet]. [contested]
- nerve above: on 5 Apr 1862 wired that he had made his arrangements to fight with his small force "without the slightest hope of success" (OR-I-11-3-p422); attacked at Savage Station late on 29 Jun with one division and two regiments (OR-I-11-2-p489-498, p494).
- experience average: USMA 1830, captain 15 years, bvt lt col, Washington garrison 1860-61 (CULLUM-601, HEIT1-magruder_john_b); no command above a battery or company before 1861.
- reputation_1861 average: brevets only (HEIT1-magruder_john_b) [single source]. Showman reputation: needs-source.
- candid: the 5 Apr 1862 wire above; his 5 Sep 1862 reply conceded "I wrote from memory" and "I learn since ... from Major-General Huger" (OR-I-11-2-p680-681).

#### stuart_james_e_b: James E. B. Stuart
- competence above: Lee's General Orders No. 74 (23 Jun 1862) announced the "brilliant exploit" of passing round McClellan's rear and praised "the courage and skill" shown (OR-I-11-1-p1036-1046) [own report plus Lee's order]; Lee used him on Jackson's left at Seven Days (OR-I-11-2-p491).
- nerve high: the 12-15 Jun 1862 ride with about 1,200 cavalry round the army with one man lost (OR-I-11-1-p1036-1046).
- experience average: USMA 1854, captain Apr 1861, frontier cavalry, wounded 1857, aide to Lee in 1859 (CULLUM-1643, HEIT1-stuart_james_e_b); no command above a company.
- reputation_1861 average: wounded in 1857 combat and volunteer aide in 1859 (CULLUM-1643) [single source]; no press found.
- insubordinate: his report says the expedition "was prosecuted farther than was contemplated in your instructions" (OR-I-11-1-p1036-1046) [own report]; Lee praised the result.

#### hill_daniel_h: Daniel H. Hill
- competence above: Johnston: "Hill's brave troops, admirably commanded and most gallantly led" forced the abatis and stormed the Seven Pines works, and Longstreet, Johnston says, spoke of Hill's conduct and courage "in the highest terms" (OR-I-11-1-p933-935) [single source]; Appletons credits Big Bethel (APPLETONS-3-p203-204).
- nerve high: his division led the assault at Seven Pines (OR-I-11-1-p933-935).
- experience average: USMA 1842, two brevets, resigned 1849, professor and school superintendent (CULLUM-1138, HEIT1-hill_daniel_h).
- reputation_1861 average: register brevets and a published algebra (CULLUM-1138) [single source].
- candid: wrote of Malvern Hill, "It was not war — it was murder" (BL-2-p394-395) [own memoir: D. H. Hill, written 1880s].
- Date conflicts: see CSV notes.

#### hill_ambrose_p: Ambrose P. Hill
- competence above: Lee wrote that Hill's "single division met this large force with the impetuous courage for which that officer and his troops are distinguished" at Gaines' Mill (OR-I-11-2-p492) [single source].
- nerve high: same passage (OR-I-11-2-p492); Mechanicsville attack (OR-I-11-2-p491).
- experience below: 1 lt from 1851 to 1861 and Coast Survey clerk (CULLUM-1345, HEIT1-hill_ambrose_p).
- reputation_1861 unknown: searched Cullum, Heitman and Wright; no contemporary judgement.
- Not tagged: quarrels with Longstreet and Jackson 1862 (needs-source).

#### ewell_richard_s: Richard S. Ewell
- competence unknown: Lee's Seven Days report names his division only in passing (OR-I-11-2-p489-498); Valley and Second Manassas reports not read.
- nerve unknown: same.
- experience average: USMA 1840, 20 years in dragoons, bvt capt, wounded 1859 (CULLUM-1029, HEIT1-ewell_richard_s).
- reputation_1861 average: one brevet (HEIT1-ewell_richard_s) [single source].

#### holmes_theophilus_h: Theophilus H. Holmes
- posts: sent to Fredericksburg by the order printed with Beauregard's June 1861 proclamation (OR-I-2-p907); styled Major General commanding Aquia District on 10 Feb 1862 (OR-I-5-p1068).
- competence unknown: Lee reports Holmes opened with artillery on 30 Jun and found batteries "advantageously posted" (OR-I-11-2-p495-496); his own report (p906) and later Trans-Mississippi record not read.
- nerve unknown: same.
- experience above: USMA 1829, major 1855, bvt maj Monterey, Superintendent of General Recruiting Service 1859-61 (CULLUM-584, HEIT1-holmes_theophilus_h).
- reputation_1861 average: one brevet (HEIT1-holmes_theophilus_h) [single source].

#### loring_william_w: William W. Loring
- posts: signed as Major General commanding the Department of Southwest Virginia on 9 Jun 1862 (OR-I-10-2-p603-604); his command was ordered to Manassas on 10 Feb 1862 (OR-I-5-p1068).
- competence unknown: no neutral judgement read; the Romney dispute is Jackson's complaint, not a finding (OR-I-5-p1053).
- nerve unknown: same.
- experience high: Mounted Rifles colonel from 1856, bvt lt col and col, lost an arm at Chapultepec, led the Department of New Mexico (HEIT1-loring_william_w, APPLETONS-4-p28-29).
- reputation_1861 above: two brevets and a sword presented by the citizens of Apalachicola (APPLETONS-4-p28-29) [single source].

#### floyd_john_b: John B. Floyd
- competence below: the Secretary of War found his and Pillow's reports "unsatisfactory" and relieved both on 11 Mar 1862 (OR-I-7-p301; WRIGHT-p50).
- nerve low: the same order asks why they "abandoned the command to their inferior officer" and how each escaped Fort Donelson (OR-I-7-p301); Pillow says Floyd and Buckner held surrender necessary (OR-I-7-p301-302) [rival: Pillow; own Floyd report unread; contested].
- experience unknown: needs-source (no regular army service in Heitman; militia and civil career unsourced).
- reputation_1861 unknown: needs-source (House inquiry into Dec 1860 Indian trust bond charges not read).
- harsh_with_civilians: his Nashville report says he forced idle able-bodied men in the city to labour (OR-I-7-p427-428) [own report].
- quarrelsome: Lee asked Wise to let the "want of consultation and concert" with Floyd "pass" (OR-I-5-p868); Floyd's 31 Aug 1861 demand for Wise's troops (OR-I-5-p823-824) [single source].
- posts: styled Major General commanding the Virginia State Line in Dec 1862 (OR-I-51-2-p657-662).
- contract_scandal: needs-source; not tagged.

#### pemberton_john_c: John C. Pemberton
- competence unknown: searched Wright and the OR index; department correspondence (OR vol 6, pp402-433) not read.
- nerve unknown: same.
- experience average: USMA 1837, Mexico with two brevets, aide to Worth 1846-49, captain 1850 (CULLUM-917, HEIT1-pemberton_john_c).
- reputation_1861 average: two brevets (HEIT1-pemberton_john_c) [single source].
- posts: the OR vol 6 summary shows him assuming command in South Carolina and Georgia on 4 Mar 1862 and assigned on 14 Mar (OR-I-6-p2-3).
- Northern birth recorded neutrally: born in Philadelphia, appointed from Virginia (WRIGHT-p16, WRIGHT-p53-54).

### Confederate generals: the West (`confederate_west`)

Page note: OR pages are read from running headers in the archive.org OCR text; a passage between two headers is cited at the nearest preceding header (possible error of one page). Own-report evidence is the author's claim about himself.

#### johnston_albert_s: Albert Sidney Johnston
- competence average: wrote on 20 Mar 1862 that he had evacuated Bowling Green, lost Nashville and was moving to join Beauregard at Corinth, defending Floyd, Pillow and Buckner as among his best officers (OR-I-7-p259, OR-I-7-p260) [own report]; Davis's message of 11 Mar 1862 found the Donelson reports unsatisfactory but did not fault Johnston (OR-I-7-p254); Grant, after studying the orders, judged him vacillating and undecided (GRANT-PM-ch25) [rival memoir: Grant] [contested]
- nerve high: called the junction with Beauregard "a hazardous experiment" and said he would accept criticism rather than defend himself (OR-I-7-p261, OR-I-7-p260) [own report]; Beauregard's report says the offensive against Grant was determined on after Johnston was advised it matched the President's expectations (OR-I-10-1-p385) [Beauregard's own report]
- experience high: West Point 1826 (CULLUM-436); colonel 2nd Cavalry 1855 and bvt brig gen 18 Nov 1857 for "ability, zeal, energy and prudence" commanding the Utah army (CULLUM-436, HEIT1-johnston_albert_s); Dept of the Pacific Jan-Apr 1861 (CULLUM-436)
- reputation_1861 high: the brevet of 1857 (HEIT1-johnston_albert_s); named second of the five full generals by rank (WRIGHT-p9); Grant says West Point contemporaries and army officers expected him to be the most formidable Confederate (GRANT-PM-ch25) [rival memoir: Grant, written 1885]
- candid: reported to Davis on Dec 1861 that 18,000-19,000 men at Bowling Green had 5,000 sick and under 13,000 effective, and called enemy estimates of his strength a gross exaggeration (OR-I-7-p746); in March 1862 admitted the Donelson blow was "most disastrous" (OR-I-7-p260)
- Assigned to command Department No. 2 by Special Orders 149 of 10 Sep 1861 (OR-I-4-p405). Not used: W. P. Johnston's Life (1878, son) and his B&L article (1:540).

#### bragg_braxton: Braxton Bragg
- competence average: Beauregard's report names him among corps commanders for "foresight and military ability" at Shiloh (OR-I-10-1-p389) [Beauregard's own report]; at Perryville his own report says he declined the field command, then ordered the attack, and withdrew next morning after claiming a heavy victory (OR-I-16-1-p1087, OR-I-16-1-p1088) [own report]; Polk's report describes a different sequence for the morning (OR-I-16-1-p1109) [contested]
- nerve average: at Shiloh his report records the order "let every order be forward" at about 4 p.m. and receipt of an order to withdraw (OR-I-10-1-p466, OR-I-10-1-p467) and Beauregard records two horses shot under him (OR-I-10-1-p389); but at Perryville he left the tactical command to Polk and retired the next morning (OR-I-16-1-p1087) [own report]
- experience above: West Point 1837, 5th; Florida war; three brevets in Mexico to lt col; resigned 1856 (CULLUM-895, HEIT1-bragg_braxton); civilian 1856-61; first Confederate brigadier general by date (WRIGHT-p48)
- reputation_1861 above: three brevets in Mexico (CULLUM-895); Louisiana public works commissioner (CULLUM-895); first of the Confederate brigadiers by date (WRIGHT-p48) [register facts only; no press comment read]
- shifts_blame: ended his Kentucky report by saying there was little or no disposition among Kentuckians to rise as promised (OR-I-16-1-p1088) [own report]
- Birth conflict: 22 Mar (WRIGHT-p11, APPLETONS-bragg_braxton) against 22 May 1817 (WRIGHT-p48). Beauregard says (B&L 1:225) he left Bragg in charge of an army "under reorganization" and that his telegram was misread (BL-1-p225) [rival memoir: Beauregard]. Pensacola report of 16 Sep 1861 shows him there (OR-I-6-p438).

#### polk_leonidas: Leonidas Polk
- competence average: Bragg's report names Polk first among those to whom the Perryville result was "mainly due" (OR-I-16-1-p1088) [own report of Bragg]; Polk's own report says he urged concentration and that the generals resolved at daylight to adopt the "defensive-offensive" (OR-I-16-1-p1109) [own report] [contested]
- nerve above: ordered the occupation of Columbus without government orders, over Walker's order to withdraw (OR-I-4-p179, OR-I-4-p180); Beauregard praises him with Bragg and Hardee for leading commands personally at Shiloh (OR-I-10-1-p389)
- experience low: West Point 1827 (8th), resigned the same year (CULLUM-477); clergyman and bishop for 34 years (CULLUM-477); no later military service
- reputation_1861 above: Bishop of Louisiana 1841-61 (CULLUM-477); appointed major general (WRIGHT-p21) [register only; Davis friendship is a pointer only, NOTE-confederate-side, needs-source]
- insubordinate: Walker telegraphed on 4 Sep 1861 "Order their prompt withdrawal from Kentucky"; Davis wired the same day "The necessity justifies the action"; Polk held Columbus (OR-I-4-p180, OR-I-4-p189 for Davis's 13 Sep limit "limited by the existence of such necessity")
- Dec 1861 letter shows an application to resign the major generalship, withdrawn after Davis's letter of 12 Nov (OR-I-7-p746) [letter not read]. Cullum lists the bishopric (CULLUM-477).

#### hardee_william_j: William J. Hardee
- competence above: Bragg names Hardee commanding the left wing as one of those to whom the Perryville result is "mainly due" (OR-I-16-1-p1088); Beauregard lists him among the Shiloh corps commanders praised for "zeal, intelligence, and energy" (OR-I-10-1-p389); acted at Iuka on 1 Apr 1862, arresting Maj Gen Crittenden for drunkenness and Brig Gen Carroll for drunkenness, incompetency and neglect, after an inspection found "wretched" discipline (OR-I-10-2-p379) [own report]
- nerve above: Beauregard records him "slightly wounded, his coat rent by balls and his horse disabled" leading his corps at Shiloh (OR-I-10-1-p389)
- experience above: West Point 1838, 26th (CULLUM-966); two brevets in Mexico (HEIT1-hardee_william_j); wrote the Rifle and Light Infantry Tactics adopted 29 Mar 1855 (CULLUM-966); Commandant of Cadets 1856-60 (CULLUM-966); lt col 1st Cavalry 28 Jun 1860 (HEIT1-hardee_william_j)
- reputation_1861 above: author of the army's adopted tactics (CULLUM-966); commandant at West Point (CULLUM-966) [register only]
- organiser: compiled the tactics manual adopted for the Army and militia (CULLUM-966); as commandant he also instructed cadets in infantry, artillery and cavalry tactics (CULLUM-966)
- Held Arkansas command in summer 1861 (assumption date needs-source; Grant reported on 1 Sep 1861 that Hardee had left Greenville and fallen back into Arkansas, OR-I-3 p.153, key not used).

#### smith_edmund_k: Edmund Kirby Smith
- competence average: his own report of Richmond, Ky. (30 Aug 1862) praises the officers and the march through a waterless country (OR-I-16-1-p934) [own report] [single source]
- nerve unknown: searched OR-I-16-1 (Smith dispatches pp.761, 934-936) and OR-I-10-2; no contemporary judgement of his nerve read.
- experience above: West Point 1845, 25th (CULLUM-1255); two brevets in Mexico (HEIT1-smith_edmund_k); assistant professor at West Point 1849-52; severely wounded in a Comanche action 13 May 1859; major 2nd Cavalry 31 Jan 1861 (CULLUM-1255)
- reputation_1861 average: brevets and a 1861 majority in the new cavalry (HEIT1-smith_edmund_k) [register only]
- infirm: Johnston's report says Smith fell severely wounded while attacking at First Manassas on 21 Jul 1861 (OR-I-2-p475) [header reads 415; probably 475]; time lost needs-source.
- Commanded the Department of East Tennessee when Davis proclaimed martial law there on 8 Apr 1862 (OR-I-10-2-p402); a dispatch of 24 Jul 1862 repeats Morgan's Kentucky news (OR-I-16-1-p761).

#### van_dorn_earl: Earl Van Dorn
- competence below: after Pea Ridge he told the Secretary of War that "a series of accidents entirely unforeseen ... and a badly-disciplined army defeated my intentions" and proposed to march on St. Louis (OR-I-8-p281, OR-I-8-p282) [own report]; the attack on Corinth failed (OR-I-17-1-p375: "Enemy received fresh re-enforcements and we could not complete the work; retired") [own report]; at Holly Springs on 20 Dec 1862 he reported destroying Union stores worth about $1,500,000 (OR-I-17-1-p503) [own report; a success against the competence direction]
- nerve high: ordered the attack on Corinth and, after the Pea Ridge repulse, planned a march on St. Louis (OR-I-8-p281, OR-I-17-1-p375) [own reports]
- experience above: West Point 1842, 52nd (CULLUM-1162); two brevets in Mexico and wounded at the Belen Gate; Comanche campaign of 1858 wounded four times; major 28 Jun 1860 (CULLUM-1162, HEIT1-van_dorn_earl)
- reputation_1861 above: made brigadier general of the Mississippi forces and Confederate colonel of cavalry in March 1861 (APPLETONS-van_dorn_earl) [single source]
- shifts_blame: see the Pea Ridge letter above (OR-I-8-p282): death of McCulloch and McIntosh, capture of Hebert, a "badly-disciplined army"
- drink_rumour: a court of inquiry (Price, Tilghman, Maury, all of his own command) convened on his request after Brig Gen Bowen's allegations, and reported that the evidence "fully disproves" the drunkenness imputation (OR-I-17-1-p413, OR-I-17-1-p459) [friendly court]

#### price_sterling: Sterling Price
- competence average: took Lexington on 20 Sep 1861 with 3,500 prisoners by his own report (OR-I-3-p185) [own report]; at Wilson's Creek McCulloch records Price had turned his command over to him days before (OR-I-3-p104); Snead, Price's staff officer, says McCulloch refused to pursue and Price resumed command (BL-1-p273) [rival memoir? partisan of Price: Snead]; Iuka and Corinth reports (OR-I-17-1-p119, OR-I-17-1-p385) [own reports]
- nerve above: pressed on to surprise Warrensburg with "greatly fatigued" men and went to Lexington (OR-I-3-p185) [own report] [single source]
- experience high: US Representative 1845-46; colonel then brigadier general of volunteers in the Mexican War (HEIT1-price_sterling, BDAC-price_sterling); Governor 1853-57 (BDAC-price_sterling)
- reputation_1861 high: ex-governor and Mexican War general (BDAC-price_sterling, HEIT1-price_sterling); president of the Missouri State Convention of 4 Mar 1861 (APPLETONS-price_sterling) [single source]
- No trait tagged. State Guard commission of 18 May 1861 rests on Appletons only (APPLETONS-price_sterling).

#### mcculloch_ben: Ben McCulloch
- competence average: his own report of Wilson's Creek admits he had meant to march at night, stopped for rain, and was still hesitating when Lyon attacked, yet won the field (OR-I-3-p104) [own report]; Snead says he refused to pursue (BL-1-p273) [rival memoir? Snead, Price staff]
- nerve above: killed in action at Pea Ridge (WRIGHT-p49); Van Dorn writes that the deaths of McCulloch and McIntosh left the right wing "without an officer to command" (OR-I-8-p282) [single source]
- experience above: captain 1st Texas Rangers and major quartermaster 1846-47 (HEIT1-mcculloch_ben); brigadier general 11 May 1861 (WRIGHT-p49)
- reputation_1861 above: first of the Confederate brigadiers for the Southwest by date (WRIGHT-p49) [register only; no contemporary judgement read]
- candid: "While still hesitating in the morning the enemy were reported advancing" (OR-I-3-p104)
- Given name: Wright prints Ben, Heitman Benjamin.

#### pillow_gideon_j: Gideon J. Pillow
- competence below: Davis wrote on 11 Mar 1862 that Pillow's and Floyd's reports were "unsatisfactory" and relieved both (OR-I-7-p254); Grant, who knew Pillow in Mexico, judged before the attack that Floyd "would yield to Pillow's pretensions" (GRANT-PM-ch22) [rival memoir: Grant]; Johnston, 20 Mar 1862, kept him among "the best officers" (OR-I-7-p259) [contested]
- nerve below: his own report says he told the council he would "neither surrender the command nor myself", passed command to Buckner, told Forrest to cut his way out and left (OR-I-7-p278, OR-I-7-p284) [own report]; Wallace says he persisted in leaving after Buckner said duty was to stay (BL-1-p425, BL-1-p426) [rival memoir: Lew Wallace]
- experience above: brigadier and major general of volunteers 1846-47, wounded at Cerro Gordo and Chapultepec (HEIT1-pillow_gideon_j, APPLETONS-pillow_gideon_j); Tennessee lawyer and planter
- reputation_1861 above: 25 votes for vice-president at the 1852 Democratic convention (APPLETONS-pillow_gideon_j); Mexican War major general (HEIT1-pillow_gideon_j)
- quarrelsome: charges of insubordination by Scott after Cerro Gordo/Mexico City led to a court of inquiry that acquitted him (APPLETONS-pillow_gideon_j) [single source]
- shifts_blame: explains that Floyd was senior and Buckner of high reputation, so he yielded his judgement to theirs (OR-I-7-p289) [own report]

#### buckner_simon_b: Simon B. Buckner
- competence above: Pillow wrote that Buckner "possessed high reputation as an officer of talent and experience" (OR-I-7-p289) [other's own report]; Bragg names Buckner among the division commanders to whom Perryville is due (OR-I-16-1-p1088) [own report of Bragg]
- nerve above: stayed to surrender the Donelson army as junior general while Floyd and Pillow left (BL-1-p425, BL-1-p426) [rival memoir: Lew Wallace]; on 16 Feb 1862 wrote Grant for an armistice and received the answer "No terms except unconditional and immediate surrender" (OR-I-7-p161)
- experience above: West Point 1844, 11th, two brevets (CULLUM-1216, HEIT1-buckner_simon_b); capt commissary 1852; Kentucky Inspector-General 1860-61 (CULLUM-1216)
- reputation_1861 average: Kentucky Inspector-General commanding Home Guards (CULLUM-1216); on 13 Sep 1861 telegraphed Davis's Adjutant-General advising withdrawal from Columbus (OR-I-4-p189) [register and one telegram]
- Kentucky neutrality telegram of 13 Sep 1861: "I regard a prompt withdrawal the only chance to unite the State" (OR-I-4-p189).

#### breckinridge_john_c: John C. Breckinridge
- competence average: reported attacking Baton Rouge on 5 Aug 1862 with not more than 2,600 men in action after sickness had cut his 4,000, and only after he was promised the ram Arkansas (OR-I-15-p73) [own report]; Beauregard lists him among the Shiloh commanders praised for energy and uses him as rear guard on 7-8 Apr (OR-I-10-1-p388, OR-I-10-1-p389)
- nerve above: "twice struck by spent balls" at Shiloh, leading the reserve (OR-I-10-1-p389)
- experience below: for a general, military experience was a volunteer major's in 1847-48 (BDAC-breckinridge_john_c); civil experience very high (Kentucky House, Congress 1851-55, Vice President 1857-61)
- reputation_1861 high: Vice President 1857-61 and Southern Democratic candidate for President in 1860 (BDAC-breckinridge_john_c)
- BDAC gives Senate service from 4 Mar 1861 until expelled 4 Dec 1861 (BDAC-breckinridge_john_c). Slaveholding needs-source.

#### crittenden_george_b: George B. Crittenden
- competence below: attacked a larger force at Mill Springs with the Cumberland at his back and no means of crossing, lost Zollicoffer and his guns (OR-I-7-p102 Johnston's dispatch, OR-I-7-p103, OR-I-7-p104) [own report]; arrested at Iuka on 1 Apr 1862 for drunkenness (OR-I-10-2-p379) [Hardee's report]
- nerve average: moved out at midnight to attack and kept fighting rather than waiting (OR-I-7-p103) [own report] [single source]
- experience above: West Point 1832, resigned 1833; captain Mounted Rifles 1846, bvt major, lieutenant colonel Dec 1856 (CULLUM-687, HEIT1-crittenden_george_b)
- reputation_1861 average: lieutenant colonel, resigned 10 Jun 1861 (HEIT1-crittenden_george_b) [register only; father's fame needs-source]
- drink_rumour: ordered to consider himself in arrest for drunkenness after Hardee's inspection (OR-I-10-2-p379) [officer finding, no court read]
- shifts_blame: report blames deserters "shamelessly" and a country without provisions (OR-I-7-p110, OR-I-7-p104)

#### lovell_mansfield: Mansfield Lovell
- competence unknown: searched OR-I-6 (his report of 26 Apr 1862, p.510; court of inquiry records pp. ~ 640-660): no finding read. needs-source
- nerve unknown: same. needs-source
- experience below: West Point 1842, 9th; bvt captain Chapultepec; resigned Dec 1854; New York street commissioner 1858-61 (CULLUM-1119, HEIT1-lovell_mansfield)
- reputation_1861 unknown: no contemporary judgement read.
- Ordered to New Orleans to relieve Twiggs by Special Orders 173 of 7 Oct 1861 (OR-I-6-p642).

#### twiggs_david_e: David E. Twiggs
- competence below: Lovell testified that Twiggs made no official report, said verbally that the department was almost defenceless and that he had been unable to get anything done (OR-I-6-p558) [single source: his successor, own account]
- nerve unknown: surrendered all posts in Texas on 18 Feb 1861; the War Department order of 1 Mar 1861 called it "treachery to the flag" (OR-I-1-p9); in 1846 he earned a brevet and a sword from Congress at Monterey (HEIT1-twiggs_david_e) [contested]
- experience high: in the army from 1812; colonel 1836; brigadier general 1846 (HEIT1-twiggs_david_e)
- reputation_1861 average: dismissed from the US Army on 1 Mar 1861 (HEIT1-twiggs_david_e, OR-I-1-p9) after a Congress sword in 1847 [contested]
- Maj Gen CSA 22 May 1861 (WRIGHT-p21); relieved at New Orleans by Special Orders 173 of 7 Oct 1861 (OR-I-6-p642). Death date conflict: July (Wright, Heitman) against September (Appletons).

#### morgan_john_h: John H. Morgan
- competence above: Smith's dispatch of 24 Jul 1862 reports Morgan had taken eleven towns and cut the Lexington-Cincinnati bridges (OR-I-16-1-p761) [single source]
- nerve high: the same raid deep in Kentucky (OR-I-16-1-p761) [single source]
- experience below: 1st lieutenant of Kentucky cavalry in the Mexican War (HEIT2-p62); manufacturer in 1861 (APPLETONS-morgan_john_h)
- reputation_1861 unknown: no source read.

#### forrest_nathan_b: Nathan B. Forrest
- competence high: led the Murfreesboro raid of 13 Jul 1862, reported by McCown as capturing two brigadier generals, 1,200 men and stores worth about $500,000 (OR-I-16-1-p809) [other's report]
- nerve high: at Donelson he asked leave to cut his way out and left with his command before the surrender (OR-I-7-p284, JORDAN-PRYOR-1868-p88, BL-1-p426 where Wallace says he "neither could nor would surrender") [partisan biography; rival memoir: Wallace]; Grant confirms Forrest escaped (GRANT-PM-ch22)
- experience low: no military service before 1861; broker and slave dealer at Memphis from 1852 and cotton planter (JORDAN-PRYOR-1868-p25) [partisan source]
- reputation_1861 unknown: no contemporary judgement read.
- Sensitive: Jordan and Pryor state that he was a dealer in slaves in Memphis from 1852 (JORDAN-PRYOR-1868-p25); no slaveholding evidence read. Fort Pillow 1864 outside the period.

#### pike_albert: Albert Pike
- competence below: his report of 14 Mar 1862 says the Choctaw, Chickasaw and Creek regiments refused to march until paid, delaying him (OR-I-8-p286) [own report]; Union commanders alleged that Native troops at Pea Ridge scalped the dead and Van Dorn's staff replied that he hoped they were misinformed (OR-I-8-p194) [contested]
- nerve unknown: no judgement read.
- experience below: captain of Arkansas volunteers in the Mexican War (HEIT2-p64); lawyer and editor
- reputation_1861 unknown: needs-source.
- Treaties with the Creek, Choctaw and Chickasaw, Seminole, Cherokee and other nations were submitted by Davis on 12 Dec 1861, who recommended rejecting provisions as unconstitutional (OR-IV-1-p785).
